# Gymdesk Roadmap

Phased implementation roadmap derived from the target ER model, adapted to the existing
multi-tenant architecture (every table gets `gym_id`; the diagram's monolithic `User` maps
onto the existing `members` + `gym_memberships` split — no new users table).

Each ticket below is a GitHub issue in `cordel-app/gymdesk`. Full scope and acceptance
criteria live in the issue bodies; this doc is the map.

Engineering quality backlog (tests, typing, helpers, member-app gap analysis) lives in
`docs/tech-debt.md` as a **map** of GitHub issues (#81–#86) — same model as this roadmap.
Agent session prompts: `docs/agent-prompts.md`. Always implement via the GitHub issue body.

## Status (2026-09-07)

plan mutable only while `status='active'`; migration 136 adds the missing `(member_nutrition_plan_id, weekday)` unique constraint; admin UI reuses `NutritionPlanTree.tsx`'s expand-in-place editor via its `apiBase` prop, now threaded through every child component instead of only the top level), #443 (Nutrition Plans: "+ New Nutrition Plan" creation flow mirroring Training Plans — scratch-vs-template modal, `POST /member-nutrition-plans`, and a shared `createNutritionPlanTx` helper also used by `/nutrition-plan-templates/:id/assign`), #501 (Nutrition Library: multiple food categories per item via a `nutrition_library_categories` + `nutrition_library_item_categories` M2M pair mirroring the existing `nutritional_qualities` pattern, replacing the old single scalar `category` column; `GET`/`POST`/`PUT`/`POST :id/duplicate` on `sellable-items.ts` extended to accept/return `professional_service_ids`/`professional_services`, validated against the requesting gym's own + global system Professional Services and persisted transactionally alongside the product write; changing type away from `sessions` clears the relationship (no confirmation — it's a catalog association, not a booking/billing record); duplicate copies links for `sessions`-type items only; admin UI adds a type-gated multi-select checkbox field (Plans/Nutrition-Library chip-checkbox pattern) to the Products inline create/edit forms and read-only/Details views), #551 (Membership Fee Benefits split into its own Promotion category, reusing the Period Benefits mechanism via a new `GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit` singleton — item hardcoded server-side to Membership Fee, generic Period Benefits excludes/rejects it, no schema change), #552 (Promotion Billing & Duration UI: dropped the "(months)" suffix from the four field labels; Example Timeline moved below all other Promotion form sections with a new "assumes monthly billing period" disclaimer; the timeline's Billing column now reflects the promotion's (unsaved, draft) Membership Fee Benefit — `GET /promotions/timeline` gained optional `membership_fee_action`/`membership_fee_value`/`membership_fee_enabled`/`membership_fee_duration_months` query params and each period's response gained `billingAction`/`billingValue`, no schema change), #553 (Promotions card UX: Example Timeline simulation now renders whenever the card is expanded — view or edit — computed from the promotion's saved values in view mode and from the unsaved edit-form draft while editing, instead of only appearing once a Billing & Duration field is touched in edit mode; shows a `timeline_empty` hint instead of hiding the section when there isn't enough data yet; Save/Cancel buttons moved from just below General to the very bottom of the edit form, after every section including the Example Timeline; no schema or API change), #625 (Limit Membership Fee Benefit duration to the Promotion duration: a Promotion Period Benefit belongs to the Promotion lifecycle and can never outlast it, so `duration_months` is capped at `free_months + paid_months + bonus_months` — Pay Beforehand excluded. Backend rejects an over-long explicit duration on `PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit`; the admin inline UI caps the input at the Promotion duration and re-constrains it live when the Promotion is shortened; the Example Timeline forecast uses the effective (clamped) duration so the benefit never reaches the Pay (regular) period even when configured too long or unbounded; migration 162 clamps existing over-long rows. Shared pure helpers `promotionDurationMonths` / `effectiveBenefitDurationMonths`), #626 (Remove Charge Benefits from Promotions: the Charge Benefits section is gone from the Promotion editor — both the edit form's per-Product action/value grid and the read-only view list — along with the frontend state, handlers and `GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/charge-benefits` calls that existed only for it, and the `promotions` namespace's Charge Benefit i18n keys. One-off / Session / Period / Membership Fee Benefits and the unrelated Membership Plans Charge Benefits section are untouched. No schema change and no backend removal: `promotion_charge_benefits` is still read at apply time by `membership-promotions.ts` (snapshot + `computeFinalPrice`) and surfaced on Assigned Plans, so the backend is not exclusively required by the removed UI), #627 (Split Promotion Editing by Section: the Promotion is no longer editable as a whole — the context-menu **Edit** action now only opens the main configuration (General, Suitable Membership Plans, Billing & Duration), and One-off / Session / Period / Membership Fee Benefits each get their own **Edit** button with their own independent Save/Cancel writing only that section's endpoint. A new `editingSection` discriminator alongside `editingId` allows exactly one section of one card to be editable at a time; every other section stays read-only and its Edit button is disabled while one is open. Creating a Promotion stays a single form (no id yet to hang per-section saves off). Frontend-only, inline/expandable UI preserved, no modal; #625's Membership-Fee-duration cap is preserved by re-clamping an over-long saved duration when the main save shortens the Promotion), #628 (Inline Assign Plan to Member with Promotion selection: the "Assign New Plan" action no longer opens a modal — `AssignNewPlanModal.tsx` is replaced by `AssignPlanInlineEditor.tsx`, rendered inline inside the plan card it supersedes, with plan picker, start date and a new **Promotions** section. Eligible Promotions come from the new `GET /promotions?membership_plan_id=` filter (`EXISTS` over `promotion_membership_plans`) so the list matches what the apply path accepts; the selection enforces the `stackable` rule live (a non-stackable pick disables every other Promotion, any pick disables the non-stackable ones, clearing re-enables all). `POST /user-memberships/:id/assign-new-plan` takes `promotion_ids` and re-validates the whole set server-side before the assignment transaction — shared pure helper `validatePromotionStacking` (`domain/promotionStacking.ts`) plus `validatePromotionSelection` for the per-Promotion eligibility checks — then applies each one through the existing `applyPromotionToMembership`. Membership Plan status workflow untouched; no schema change), #630 (Rename "Close" to "Cancel": the Assigned Plan context-menu action reads **Cancel** in every locale — label-only, the action still posts to `POST /user-memberships/:id/close`, still runs the two-step confirm (plain, then the unused-value warning) and still maps onto the `cancelled` status. Because the action's own name became "Cancel", the confirm dialogs no longer label their dismiss button with the generic `cancel` key — new `action_close_confirm` / `action_close_dismiss` keys give the two buttons distinct labels. Frontend-only, no API or schema change), #632 (Collapsible Theme color groups: every group in the Theme editors' **Colors** section (Application, Cards, Text, Separators, Inputs, Header, Sidebar, Navigation, Buttons, Status, Links, Tables, Calendar) now renders as a collapsible card — a full-width header `<button>` carrying the group's existing label, `aria-expanded` and a rotating `▾`, with the group's controls hidden while collapsed. All groups start collapsed. Open state is a local `Set` of group keys in `ThemeColorsEditor`, kept separate from the token draft, so groups toggle independently (not an accordion) and collapsing can never change a color value. Presentation-only: same color pickers, same inline `ThemeAdvancedSection`, same Calendar contrast report, same read-only handling for Base Themes, no new translation keys. Frontend-only, no API or schema change), #633 (Promotions — new `only_applicable_for_new_members` boolean on the main Promotion configuration: migration 163 adds `promotions.only_applicable_for_new_members tinyint(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` after `stackable`, so existing Promotions are backfilled to `true` by the column default; `promotions.ts` carries it through the list SELECT, POST (defaults to `1` when omitted or null), PUT (`IF(?, ?, …)` partial-update semantics, matching `stackable`) and `POST /:id/duplicate`; the admin Promotions page renders it as a checkbox directly below "Stackable with other promotions", checked by default on create, and the Promotion detail modal shows it read-only. Store-and-display only — Promotion eligibility, stacking, the apply path and the Assign-Plan-to-Member flow are deliberately unchanged, and the `user_membership_promotions` snapshot does not capture it yet; a follow-up ticket implements the behaviour), #637 (Products column alignment: the list's column headers and every collapsed row now render from one shared `LIST_COLUMNS` definition in `financials/sellable-items/page.tsx` — a CSS-grid template (fixed px tracks, the Name column the only flexible one) used by both, replacing the flex rows whose per-cell `minWidth` let a long name or a long "created by" widen its cell and push every column after it out of line with the header. Values ellipsis inside their own track, the chevron and the ⋮ menu share one Actions cell (new `col_actions` label in en/es/ca, the only new key), and the header + rows sit in a single `overflow-x: auto` wrapper with a computed `LIST_MIN_WIDTH`, so a narrow viewport scrolls horizontally instead of dropping columns. Presentation-only: same columns, same order, same data, same actions, no API or schema change), #638 (Finance Dashboard — Membership Plan cards: the `Finance → Dashboard` page (`[locale]/financials/page.tsx`, until now a "coming soon" placeholder) renders one card per Membership Plan with its name, lifecycle status badge and the number of Assigned Members. New read-only router `api/src/api/financials-dashboard.ts` mounted at `/financials/dashboard` under the `financials` **group** flag (not `financials.plans`, so the Dashboard survives the Plans page being switched off); its single `GET /membership-plans` groups `user_memberships` per plan. A plan is shown when it is `lifecycle_status='active'` or still has at least one assignment, so retired plans members are still on stay visible while empty non-active plans disappear. An assignment counts while it is anything other than `cancelled`/`expired` under the #410 date-aware projection (so the count agrees with the Assigned Plans list), and assignments of soft-deleted members are excluded; each assignment counts once. No migration, no write path, no Membership Plan / Assigned Plan behaviour change. New `api/src/test/financials-dashboard.test.ts` (14 cases: auth, role, tenant isolation, visibility rules, counting invariants); new `financials_dashboard` i18n namespace in en/es/ca), #639 (Created and Next Payment on Billing Events: `GET /payments/billing-events` rows gain `created_at` (the ledger row's creation instant as a full ISO datetime; `null` on projected rows, which aren't persisted) and `next_payment_date` (`YYYY-MM-DD` of `user_memberships.next_billing_date` for the event's membership, `null` when the event has no membership, the membership is no longer `active`, or nothing is scheduled). The admin Billing Events table renders both as new `Created` / `Next Payment` columns in `DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm`, with `—` for missing data; `next_billing_date` is a DATE column so the scheduled day is rendered at `00:00` rather than parsed as an instant and shifted by the viewer's timezone. Also fixes the future-row projection, which seeded its rolling window with `String(next_billing_date).slice(0, 10)` — mysql2 returns DATE columns as JS `Date` objects, so scheduled rows carried a `billing_date` of `"Wed Oct 21"` instead of an ISO date (rendered as `Invalid Date`, and mis-sorted/mis-filtered against `from`/`to`); a new `toDateOnly()` helper normalises both representations. No migration, no billing-logic, payment-execution or event-status change; new `col_created`/`col_next_payment` i18n keys in en/es/ca; 6 new cases in `payments-billing-events.test.ts`), #642 (View Audit Log from Member Details: the Members → Details modal gains a **View Audit Log** button that closes the modal and navigates to Configuration → Audit Log already filtered to that member — `/{locale}/audit?entity_type=member&entity_id=<id>`. `AuditLogView` now seeds its `entity_type` / `entity_id` / `entity_name` filters from the query string, and a new **Entity ID** filter box makes the applied filter visible and clearable; `GET /audit-logs` gains a matching exact-match `entity_id` param (served by the existing `(gym_id, entity_type, entity_id)` index — no migration). The button is hidden for non-admins and when the `system.audit` feature flag is off, mirroring the sidebar's rule. New `audit.filter_entity_id*` and `members.action_view_audit_log` i18n keys in en/es/ca; new `audit-logs.test.ts` (8 cases: auth, entity_id filtering, exact match, tenant isolation)), #629 stage 1 (Billing Simulation engine: a read-only, never-persisted forecast of what a Member will actually be charged — `GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/billing-simulation` returns the concrete dates, the regular price, the Promotion/Benefit that changed it and the resulting amount, consolidated across every simulated assignment of the Member. Member-level rather than per Assigned Plan, per the ticket thread's Q4 answer, so the engine lands once and renders where #634 wants it. Pure engine in `domain/billingSimulation.ts` (sections one-off / year / monthly / 4-week, plus weekly / session / other for cadences the catalogue can produce; Free and Bonus promotional periods waive the fee, Pay/Prepaid apply the Membership Fee Benefit via `computePromotionTimeline`, granted Products are projected at their own `billing_frequency` with 4 weeks as 28 days); loader + router in `api/src/api/billing-simulation.ts`; `loadPromotionApplications` is now exported from `user-memberships.ts` and also carries each Promotion's name and Free/Paid/Bonus/Pay-Beforehand duration. The horizon runs until every item has been charged once at its regular price, capped at 36 months (`truncated`), and charges a year or more out are flagged `price_may_change`. Plan Charge Benefits are deliberately excluded (being decommissioned); Additional Periodic Services (#631) plug into the same engine as stage 2. No migration, no writes — nothing is persisted. New `BILLING SIMULATION` section on the Member expanded row (`MemberBillingSimulation.tsx`), outside the Membership Plan cards; new `members.billing_simulation_*` i18n keys in en/es/ca; 23 unit tests in `billing-simulation.test.ts`, 19 integration cases in `user-memberships.test.ts`, 8 structural cases in the admin app's `member-billing-simulation.test.ts`), #635 (stage 1 — Membership Plans aligned with the Promotion benefit structure: migration 173 adds `free_months`/`paid_months`/`bonus_months` to `membership_plans` and the three Product-keyed benefit tables `membership_plan_session`/`_oneoff`/`_periodical`, the same shape migration 155 gave Promotions; new `GET`/`PUT /membership-plans/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` with the Promotion endpoints' exact contract (replace-all `{items:[{gym_charge_id,quantity}]}`, category checked via `classifySellableItem()`, an already-attached item survives going inactive); `PUT /membership-plans/:id` accepts the three duration fields, clearing one back to NULL rather than 0; `/:id/duplicate` carries both; new `BILLING & DURATION`, `ONE-OFF`, `SESSION` and `PERIOD BENEFITS` sections on the Plans page, each with its own Edit/Save/Cancel and no modal, sharing the new `components/SellableItemBenefits.tsx` editor; no Membership Fee Benefits (Promotion-only) and no billing change — Included Services and Charge Benefits stay load-bearing until stage 4; 60 integration cases in `membership-plan-benefits.test.ts`, 17 structural cases in the admin app's `plans-benefit-sections.test.ts`), #635 (stage 2 — the Assigned Plan keeps its own snapshot of the commercial configuration it was assigned with: migration 174 adds `free_months`/`paid_months`/`bonus_months` + `recurring_billing_interval`/`recurring_billing_unit` + `membership_fee_price` to `user_memberships`, the three benefit tables `user_membership_session`/`_oneoff`/`_periodical` (quantity plus the item's frozen `item_name`/`item_type`/`item_billing_frequency`/`unit_price`/`currency`, `gym_charge_id` FK deliberately without CASCADE so the record outlives a retired item), pricing columns on migration 156's `user_membership_promotion_*_snapshot` tables and on `user_membership_services`, and a non-destructive backfill of existing rows from the live catalogue (the #635 Q3 answer allowed hard-deleting them instead); `snapshotAssignedPlan()` in the new `api/src/api/assigned-plan-snapshot.ts` runs inside the assignment transaction of all three entry points (`POST /user-memberships`, `/:id/assign-new-plan`, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`), `snapshotPromotionGrants()` finally writes the migration-156 tables when a Promotion is applied, and attaching an Additional Periodic Service captures the item's price; `GET /user-memberships/:id` serves it as `snapshot` (with `snapshot_captured` marking a pre-migration row) and each service row carries a `snapshot` object; billing and the Billing Simulation still read the live catalogue — that cutover, with the fallback for pre-snapshot rows, is stage 3; 16 integration cases in `assigned-plan-snapshot.test.ts`), #635 (stage 3 — billing reads the Assigned Plan snapshot: the Billing Simulation now takes the regular Membership Fee, the billing cadence, the Plan's One-off/Session/Period Benefits, each applied Promotion's grants and each attached service's price from the assignment's own rows, falling back to the live catalogue only for an assignment that captured none; the Plan's benefit sections become billable lines for the first time — charged, not free — and a Product carried by both the Plan and a Promotion is merged into one stream so it is never billed twice; the shared `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE` SQL fragment (`COALESCE(um.recurring_billing_*, bp.recurring_billing_*)`) puts the nightly run, the Billing Events projection, the Billing Events page's scheduled rows, the Payments dashboard, the post-payment schedule advance and the first-payment webhook on the frozen cadence, each LEFT JOINing `billing_policies` so a deleted policy no longer strands assignments; no migration; fixes a pre-existing defect in `POST /billing/run` where `insertId` was read off `rows`, so every settled charge threw, was logged as a provider error, rolled back and left `next_billing_date` unmoved; 15 integration cases in `assigned-plan-snapshot-billing.test.ts`, 7 engine unit cases and 2 billing-run regression cases), #635 (stage 4, part 1 — **Charge Benefits retired**: migration 176 drops `plan_charge_benefits` (089/091/145) and its assignment-time snapshot `user_membership_charge_benefits` (130), answering Q4's "clean up completely these legacy structure"; `GET`/`PUT /membership-plans/:id/charge-benefits` are gone, `enrichPlan` no longer serves `charge_benefits` and no longer copies them on assign or duplicate, `GET /user-memberships/:id` drops the field, and the #485 Plan Billing Forecast is the plan fee and its cadence alone (`benefitLines: []`) — nothing else could move its total. Admin: the `CHARGE BENEFITS` section, its state/handlers and every `plans.cb_*`/`forecast_benefit_*` key in en/es/ca are removed; the Details modal's Benefits count is now the three Product-keyed sections. Nothing billed off these tables after stage 3, so no assignment changes what it charges. 10 integration cases in `charge-benefits-retired.test.ts`, 6 structural cases in `plans-charge-benefits-removed.test.ts`), #635 (stage 4, part 2 — **Included Services retired**: migration 177 drops `plan_allowances` (061), after the issue answered where the relation belongs — *"Activity type should flag [which] membership plans are enabled to attend [a] certain activity type"* — which is `activity_type_eligible_plans` (#481/migration 139), already editable from the Activity Types page. `GET`/`POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE /membership-plans/:id/allowances` are gone, `enrichPlan` no longer serves `allowances` or copies them on duplicate, `GET /user-memberships/:id` and the Member membership configuration drop `activity_allowances`, and the Close action's unused-value check is back to the pending billing event alone. `plan-allowances.ts` is deleted: its activity-type gate is now `activity-eligibility.ts`'s alone, its **center coverage** check moved unchanged to the new `plan-center-access.ts`, and the **class-package claim** it delegated to `package-credits.ts` is made by the eligibility gate itself via the new `tryClaimPackageCredit()` — so a member whose plan does not cover an activity still pays for it out of prepaid sessions, and the two hooks no longer depend on registration order. Admin: the `INCLUDED SERVICES` section with its state, handlers and `plans.*` allowance keys in en/es/ca is removed; the Assigned Plan card's `BENEFITS` section now renders the assignment's own #635 snapshot (One-off / Session / Period) instead of allowance usage, and `allowance_exhausted` leaves the staff-override list since nothing emits it. The one behaviour genuinely dropped is the per-window `session_count` cap, which eligibility has no equivalent for and §1 forbade relocating. 18 integration cases in `included-services-retired.test.ts`, 9 structural cases in the admin app's `included-services-removed.test.ts`), #635 (stage 5 — **the Membership Fee Benefit gets its own table**, answering the issue thread's *"information under promotion_session_benefits, promotion_period_benefits, promotion_membership_fee_benefits and promotion_one_off_benefits is no longer reading from promotion_charge_benefits"* and *"I'd prefer to migrate it to a new and clean structure"*: migration 178 creates `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` — one row per Promotion, no `charge_type_id` and no Product, since the item was never a choice — migrates into it the `promotion_period_benefits` row whose charge type was `membership_fee` (#551) and, for a Promotion configured before #626 removed the editor, its `promotion_charge_benefits` row on a membership-fee item (an unbounded benefit, which is how it always priced), then drops all three legacy `charge_types`-keyed tables: `promotion_charge_benefits` (020/092/102/145), `promotion_period_benefits` (021/070/093/102/144/162) and the long-unused `promotion_included_benefits` (102). `GET`/`PUT /promotions/:id/charge-benefits` are gone (the section had no UI since #626); `/membership-fee-benefit` reads and upserts the new table and no longer returns a `charge_type_*` triplet; `computeFinalPrice()` applies one benefit per Promotion instead of a Charge Benefit and a Period Benefit in turn; the apply-time snapshot carries `membership_fee_benefits` instead of the three `charge_types` arrays, with `membershipFeeBenefitsFromSnapshot()` reading the pre-stage-5 shape so an existing application still prices identically; `MembershipFeeBenefit` collapses from a two-variant union to one duration-gated shape. Assigned Plans are untouched — since stage 3 billing reads their own snapshot. 6 unit cases in `membership-fee-benefit-snapshot.test.ts`, plus the Promotion/apply suites updated to the new table), #635 (stage 6 — **the Assigned Plan's snapshot is shown and edited section by section** (§9/§10/§15): the Assigned Plans expanded row gains the Membership Plan's own structure — **Billing & Duration** (Free Period / Paid Duration / Bonus Duration, plus the frozen cadence and regular Membership Fee) above **One-off / Session / Period Benefits** — each with its own Edit/Save/Cancel and no modal, reusing the shared `SellableItemBenefits` editor the Plans and Promotions pages already use. New `PUT /user-memberships/:id/billing-duration` and `GET`/`PUT /user-memberships/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` take the same replace-all `{ items: [{ gym_charge_id, quantity }] }` contract as the Plan-side sections but write the assignment's own rows: the source Plan, its other assignments and the Products are untouched (§15), and since stage 3 that is exactly what the assignment bills, so the Billing Simulation moves with the edit. A line already in a section keeps the price it was agreed at and only changes quantity — a newly added line freezes the item as it is now (§17) — and a retired item stays saveable, while a new one must be active and of the section's category. An assignment that captured no snapshot (a pre-migration-174 row the backfill could not reach) is materialised from what it resolves live *before* the edit applies (`materialiseAssignedPlanSnapshot`), so replacing one section cannot blank the other two; a cancelled or expired assignment is read-only. No migration — the tables are stage 2's. 22 integration cases in `assigned-plan-snapshot-editing.test.ts`, 12 structural cases in the admin app's `assigned-plan-configuration.test.ts`), #635 (stage 7 — **the Assigned Plan's applied Promotions are read and priced from their own snapshot** (§16): the Promotions section of the expanded row becomes one expandable card per application — checkbox, name, applied at, applied by, StatusBadge — opening onto the agreed window, Billing & Duration, the Membership Fee Benefit and the Products it granted, every line at its frozen price; a revoked application reads as `inactive`, its checkbox cleared and its card not expandable, and clearing a standing one's checkbox revokes it (the existing DELETE, behind a confirmation), which recomputes the price and moves the Billing Simulation with it. `fetchAppliedPromotions()` gains `applied_by_name`, `display_status` (new pure `domain/promotionApplicationStatus.ts`: inactive / expired against the *snapshot's* window / active — "incompatible" cannot persist, since a Promotion that does not stack is refused at apply time) and the three grant arrays, loaded through the same `loadPromotionGrantSnapshots()` the Billing Simulation uses, falling back to the Promotion's live benefits only for an application that predates the snapshot flow. `computeFinalPrice()` reads each application's Membership Fee Benefit out of its snapshot instead of joining `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` live — the last path by which editing a Promotion could still move an existing assignment's price, since `final_price` is recomputed at every apply/revoke (§13); the `duration_months` gate stays in SQL so month arithmetic clamps as before. No migration — everything read was already captured by stages 2/5. 15 integration cases in `assigned-plan-promotions.test.ts`, 7 unit cases in `promotion-application-status.test.ts`, 13 structural cases in the admin app's `assigned-plan-promotions.test.ts`), #635 (stage 8 — **the Assigned Plan's Billing & Duration finally bills** (§7 + the thread's Q2 answer): `free_months`/`paid_months`/`bonus_months` have been stored on a Plan since stage 1 and frozen onto every assignment since stage 2, but nothing read them — a Plan sold with a free first month billed its full fee on day one. New pure `api/src/domain/planDuration.ts` classifies a date as `free_plan`/`pay_plan`/`bonus_plan`/`pay_regular`, counted from the assignment's own `starts_at` (not snapped to the first of its month, the way a Promotion's illustrative timeline is) with every boundary measured from that anchor in one step so `advanceBillingDate`'s end-of-month clamping can't reorder them; the Billing Simulation waives the Membership Fee in the Free Period and the Bonus Duration, reporting a `source: 'membership_plan'` benefit line carrying the period, and charges the regular price in the Paid Duration — where the projection stops, since that *is* the regular charge, unless a Bonus Duration behind it still has to be shown. **The Promotion wins any overlap** (Q2: "in case of conflict, prioritize the promotion"): where an applied Promotion governs the date — inside its own timeline or applying its Membership Fee Benefit — it decides the fee alone, and the Plan's own period applies only where no Promotion does (including after one is revoked). The durations are read from the assignment's own columns, falling back to the Plan's live ones only for an assignment that captured no snapshot at all — the same all-or-nothing rule as `loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation`, because the columns are nullable and a per-column COALESCE would let a Free Period *added to the Plan later* reach an existing assignment (§13). Only the Membership Fee is waived: the Plan's Period Benefits are still charged, as a Promotion's free months leave them charged too. No migration, and the nightly run is unchanged — free/paid/bonus shape the projection, exactly as a Promotion's always have. 11 unit cases in `plan-duration.test.ts`, 14 engine unit cases in `billing-simulation.test.ts`, 12 integration cases in `assigned-plan-duration-billing.test.ts`, 2 structural cases in the admin app's `member-billing-simulation.test.ts`), #635 (stage 9 — **a Promotion can be deselected and selected again** (the thread's Q2 answer: Promotions are *"selectable and deselectable adjusting automatically the simulation"*): stage 7 shipped only the deselect half, because `ump_unique_pair` (migration 022) reserved `(user_membership_id, promotion_id)` across every status, so a revoked application held the pair for good and a second apply answered 409. Migration 183 narrows the uniqueness to the applications still *standing* — a `standing_promotion_key` VIRTUAL generated column, non-NULL only while `status = 'applied'`, under `ump_one_standing_per_promotion`, the same shape migration 164 gives Additional Periodic Services — after creating the plain index the dropped unique key was also serving for the `user_membership_id` foreign key. A re-apply writes a **new** application rather than resurrecting the revoked one: the row owns the snapshot it was agreed with (§16) and its `[applied_at, revoked_at]` window tags the Billing Events ledger, so the spent one stays as history and the new one snapshots the Promotion as it stands today — a Promotion repriced in between moves the new application only (§13). Every eligibility rule stays on the unchanged apply path; the one addition is an explicit "a standing application already holds this pair" check, so the database's key surfaces as a 409 with a message. New pure `canReapplyPromotion()` (`domain/promotionApplicationStatus.ts`) decides the per-application `can_reapply` the card reads — inactive, nothing of the same Promotion standing, and the Promotion active and inside its own window today — and `AssignedPlanPromotions.tsx` ticks a spent checkbox back through the apply endpoint behind its own confirmation, which says the Promotion is agreed as it stands today. The migration also moves `applied_at`'s default from migration 022's `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` (the server's session time zone) to `UTC_TIMESTAMP()`, the clock the revoke path stamps `revoked_at` with: the two bound the same window, and since stage 9 a replacement application's `applied_at` is compared against the `revoked_at` of the one it replaced. 7 unit cases in `promotion-application-status.test.ts`, 17 integration cases in `promotion-reapply.test.ts`, 4 structural cases in the admin app's `assigned-plan-promotions.test.ts`), #635 (stage 10 — **the Member sees the snapshot too**: `GET /me/membership`, the Member app's My Membership page, was the last surface reading an existing assignment's commercial configuration live. Its `BENEFITS` list came from `membership_plan_benefits` — migration 006's plan-keyed benefit vocabulary, joined to `benefit_types`, keyed to the *Plan* and written by no code in the project's lifetime — and its billing cadence straight from `billing_policies`, so a Plan re-cadenced after enrollment moved the dates the member was shown (§13/§14). Both now read the assignment's own rows: the cadence through `ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`, like every other billing query, and the benefits through `loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation()`, so the Member reads the same Products the Billing Simulation and the nightly run bill, at the quantity, frequency and price frozen with them — with the Plan's live sections as the fallback only for an assignment that captured no snapshot at all (the same all-or-nothing rule as stage 3). Migration 184 drops `membership_plan_benefits` now that its last reader is gone; `benefit_types` stays, a global vocabulary with its own read-only router, so retiring it is a separate decision. New `api/src/api/billing-run-pricing.ts` decides, for the date being billed (`next_billing_date`, not "today"), whether the fee is waived, by calling the simulation's own `resolveMembershipFee()` — exported for the purpose, so the run and what the member was shown cannot disagree, precedence rule included (a Promotion that governs the date decides alone). A waived cycle calls no payment provider and writes no `payment_requests` row: it records a `waived_billing` billing event (amount 0, `source = 'system'`, `notes` naming the period — `free_plan` / `bonus_plan` / `free_promotion` / `bonus_promotion`, migration 185 widens the `event_type` CHECK) and advances `next_billing_date` exactly as a charged cycle would, leaving `last_billed_at` untouched; the run answers with a fourth counter, `waived`. A cycle that is *not* waived is charged `final_price` unchanged — the remaining §16 gap (a Promotion's Membership Fee Benefit that has run out keeps discounting) needs `computeFinalPrice`, the simulation engine and `computeMembershipFeePriceAt` reconciled first, and charging a member *more* on the strength of their disagreement is not a billing run's call. An assignment that captured no snapshot reads its Plan's live durations, all-or-nothing (§13); a €0 `final_price` is treated as waived rather than sent to the provider as a zero-amount MIT. `advanceBillingDate` moved to its own leaf module `domain/billingDate.ts` (the run importing the engine would otherwise close an import cycle), re-exported from `api/billing.ts` so no caller changed. 9 integration cases in `billing-run-waived.test.ts`, 11 unit cases in `membership-fee-resolution.test.ts`, 9 structural cases in the admin app's `billing-event-waived-label.test.ts` and 4 in the member app's), #635 (stage 12 — **one date-aware Membership Fee rule, everywhere**: the thread's stage 12 answer (a) — "a Promotion's own Free/Paid/Bonus months determine when its Membership Fee Benefit ends" — settled the last §16 gap. Three paths priced a fee and only one was right: `final_price` (what the run charged) and `computeMembershipFeePriceAt()` (the Billing Events projection) applied a Promotion's benefit for as long as the application stood, gated only by its own `duration_months`, while the Billing Simulation and My Membership stopped it at the end of the Promotion's timeline, so the same cycle was *shown* one price and *charged* another, in both directions. `resolveMembershipFee()` is now the only implementation: `computeMembershipFeePriceAt()` delegates to it (and gained the assignment's own Billing & Duration, so a Free Period shows €0 there too), `computeFinalPrice()` resolves the cycle `next_billing_date` names rather than discounting a date-less number, `GET /me/membership` prices each of its two `upcoming_payments` on its own date instead of repeating `final_price`, and `POST /billing/run` charges what that resolver returns. `MembershipFeeBenefit` / `AppliedPromotionForBilling` / `promotionCoversDate` moved to a new leaf module `domain/promotionApplication.ts` (carrying the Promotion's months now that every caller needs them) to invert the import edge that had kept the two rules apart, re-exported from `domain/assignedPlanBillingEvents.ts`; `regularMembershipFee()` moved to `api/user-memberships.ts` so the number every path discounts *from* is resolved by one chain too (snapshot fee → Plan price window → non-zero `base_price` → `final_price`). Because the correction *raises* the charge of a member whose promotional months already elapsed, the two money-moving writers — `computeFinalPrice()` and the run — are gated on `billing.date_aware_membership_fee`, which migration 186 seeds **disabled** (its `down()` deliberately keeps the row: a missing key reads as enabled). While it is off nothing about a charge changes and the impact is reported instead: the run logs every assignment whose resolved price differs and counts them in a fifth response counter (`drift`), and the new `GET /user-memberships/reports/membership-fee-drift` (`api/src/api/membership-fee-drift.ts`, read-only, tenant-scoped) lists each affected assignment with its stored price, regular fee, promotion timelines, benefit end date (`promotionTimelineEndsOn()`), newly resolved price and the difference. A manual discount still in force (`discount_reason`) outranks the catalogue fee, so this can never undo a discount a human agreed. 15 integration cases in `billing-run-date-aware-fee.test.ts`, 3 in `me-membership-snapshot.test.ts`, 7 unit cases in `membership-fee-resolution.test.ts` and 8 rewritten in `assignedPlanBillingEvents.test.ts`. **Remaining before the epic closes**: review the drift report on real data and switch the flag on), #635 (stage 13 — **Billing & Duration is the Plan's only billing section, and gains a Pre-paid Duration**: the thread's final-model comment asked for the Initial Billing / Initial Service / Recurring Service pairs to go and for all consumers to move off them. and its assignment snapshot `user_memberships.pay_beforehand_months`, the Promotion's own column (migration 141) with the same 0..paid_months bound, validated on both the Plan (`PUT /membership-plans/:id`) and the assignment (`PUT /user-memberships/:id/billing-duration`, rejected inside the transaction so a materialised snapshot rolls back with it). `domain/planDuration.ts` /user-memberships/reports/membership-fee-drift`, which a gym admin cannot read. **Payments → Membership Fee Drift** is that page: read-only (one GET, no write control, no flag switch), the shared Migration 191 deletes both flag rows and drops `user_memberships.final_price` the flag helper and the legacy branch of the run's pricing go with them and **Payments → Membership Fee Drift** — endpoint (`membership-fee-drift.ts`) page nav entry locale keys and the run's `drift` counter — is deleted empty by construction once nothing is stored to drift from. `billing-run-pricing.ts` becomes the shared **`membership-fee-pricing.ts`**: `priceMembershipFeeOn(row, date)` a batch `priceMembershipFeesFor()` `currentCycleDate()` (what "now" means: `max(next_billing_date, today)` never before `starts_at`) `currentMembershipFee(s)()` and one `FEE_ASSIGNMENT_COLUMNS` fragment so the nightly run the Assigned Plans list and card the create/renew/lifecycle responses apply/revoke `GET /members/:id/membership-configuration` `GET /me/membership` and the Payments dashboard's projected rows all resolve the same number and report it as a computed `membership_fee`. A **negotiated** fee now lives in `membership_fee_price` (`POST /user-memberships` and `/:id/assign-new-plan` take that field with `discount_reason`; `PUT /:id` takes no price at all — editing one is a snapshot edit so it goes through `PUT /:id/billing-duration`) and `POST /membership-plans/:id/pricing/apply-to-assigned-plans` writes that column too — it wrote only `base_price`/`plan_price_id`/`final_price` before none of which the resolver reads first so under date-aware pricing it would have changed nothing an assignment bills — materialising each affected assignment's snapshot first. The migration's backfill is narrow (it writes the fee only where the old chain would have returned `final_price` anyway) and materialises an uncaptured candidate in full before writing it because `membership_fee_price` is one of the disjuncts that define `has_billing_snapshot` and the fallback it turns off is all-or-nothing), #772 (Personal Membership Fee Benefit on an Assigned Membership Plan: `No benefit` / `% discount` stored as `user_memberships.personal_fee_benefit_action` + `personal_fee_benefit_value` (migration 192, no CHECK on that table — `toPersonalFeeBenefit()` clamps 0..100 instead), edited through `PUT /user-memberships/:id/fee-benefit` and its own MEMBERSHIP FEE BENEFIT section on the Assigned Plan card. Applied last and on every cycle by `resolveMembershipFee()` via a required `personalFeeBenefit` on `MembershipFeeContext`, so it stacks with an applied Promotion's own benefit and survives it — the one fee benefit bounded by nothing. Deliberately outside the Assigned Plan snapshot: no materialisation, no effect on `snapshot_captured`, and not carried onto a superseding assignment), #777 (Migration 191 carries a staff-negotiated price that lived only in `final_price`: a second backfill pass, after the last-resort one and before the `DROP COLUMN`, moves `final_price` into `membership_fee_price` for an assignment created with a price override before stage 15 — the override went to the dropped column while `snapshotAssignedPlan()` (or migration 174's backfill, for an older row) froze the catalogue price. Guards: non-empty `discount_reason`, `membership_fee_price <> final_price`, non-terminal status, no Promotion application in any status (a standing one has its discount baked into the total, a revoked one had `final_price` recomputed from scratch — typically to 0 under the legacy rule — so the agreed number is in neither column; those rows are logged by the migration and are a go-to-production checklist item); `discount_expires_at` is not a guard because the column means what was agreed, and a lapsed agreement resolves the catalogue price through `ignoreFrozenFee` anyway), #778 (the nightly Billing Run workflow reports honestly: `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml` reads the run's response instead of discarding it and goes red on `failed > 0`, on a body with no counters, or on a non-2xx including the guard's 429 — a red run is the notification; `/billing/cleanup` still runs when the charge step failed, and `recurring-booking-run.yml` gets the same rule with `skipped` staying green), #780 (the nightly run guard is a calendar rule: migration 193 turns `billing_run_log` and `recurring_booking_run_log` from `last_run_at` singletons into histories — one row per run with `run_date`, `status`, `started_at`/`finished_at` and the run's own counters — and both `POST /billing/run` and an unscoped `POST /recurring-bookings/run` now refuse only when a run for today's UTC date has already **completed**. That fixes the two defects of the 23-hour window: a cron GitHub fired late no longer skips a day, and a run that crashed no longer locks one (its row is closed as `failed` from the route's `catch`, and a row left `in_progress` past `STALE_RUN_MINUTES`=30 is taken over by the next claim). Overlap is still prevented — a fresh `in_progress` row answers `429` — while "today's run already completed" answers `200 { skipped_reason: 'already_completed_today', run_date, …zeroed counters }`, deliberately not a 429, so the second daily attempt #781 adds is a green no-op and #778's body parse keeps working; both workflows learned to report that as the no-op it is. The decision is pure in `domain/runGuard.ts` (unit-tested: a late cron, a crashed run, a stale lock) behind `infra/run-log.ts`, whose `claimRun()` reads and inserts under one short `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` transaction that never spans the run itself), #781 (the nightly Billing Run gets a second scheduled attempt: `.github/workflows/billing-run.yml` fires `POST /billing/run` at 06:00 **and** 10:00 UTC, so a first attempt GitHub delayed or dropped is still charged the same day. Nothing else changed — #780's guard makes the second attempt a no-op (`skipped_reason: 'already_completed_today'`, reported green by the step #778 wrote), so a normal day still produces exactly one set of charges, and `POST /billing/cleanup` running twice is idempotent. §3 decided: `recurring-booking-run.yml` keeps its single 03:00 schedule, because a dropped booking night is recovered by the next one re-projecting the rolling window from *now*), #785 (nightly Billing Run dunning: a rejected recurring charge now escalates instead of repeating every night for ever. The first provider rejection bumps `user_memberships.failed_attempts`/`last_failed_at` (migration 194) and is retried on the **next run day**, since `next_billing_date` never moved; the second consecutive rejection of the same due cycle pauses the assignment (`recordStatusChange`, `active → paused`, `source = 'system'`), which drops it out of the run's `WHERE status = 'active'` — no third provider fee, no third `failed_billing` row. The count advances per run *day*, so a second rejection on a date the assignment was already rejected on (#781's 10:00 UTC attempt after a crashed 06:00 one) records itself without escalating. A settled or waived cycle, the payment webhook's `completed` branch, a staff Retry that succeeds, a Manual payment and any transition back to `active` all clear the count; a provider exception (`provider_error`) and a missing stored card (`no_payment_method`) never count towards the pause, because neither is a decline. The rule is pure in `domain/billingDunning.ts` (unit-tested) and the run reports a new `paused` counter. Per the ticket the member is **not** notified and reactivation stays explicit (`POST /user-memberships/:id/reactivate`)), #788 (Let a member replace their stored card without being charged: a **card verification** — `PaymentProvider.createCardVerificationRequest()`, which takes no amount at all, sent to Monei as `amount: 0` + `transactionType: 'VERIF'` + `generatePaymentToken: true` — replaces the card on file without charging, where the only way before was the Start payment button's full membership fee, and only on a cycle that owed one. The verification needs a `payment_requests` row for its `page_token`/`provider_order`, so migration 195 widens `chk_payment_requests_source` with `card_update`, makes `charge_type_id` nullable (a verification bills nothing, and a `card_verification` charge type would put a non-charge in the cash-payment picker) and adds `payment_methods.updated_at`, which is what "card stored since" now reads. The webhook's card-update branch upserts the new token and does nothing else — no `payment_recorded` event, no `next_billing_date`, none of #785's dunning counters cleared, since no money arrived; a rejected or token-less verification leaves the previous card in place. `GET /members`' `payment_status`, `GET /payment-requests` and `GET /me/payment-requests` all exclude the new source, so a verification can never read as an unpaid fee. New staff router `payment-methods.ts` (read-only card + **Send replace-card link**) and `/me/payment-method` (GET / POST replace-requests / DELETE) share `api/card-updates.ts`; the rules are pure in `domain/storedCards.ts`. Removal is refused (`409 billable_membership`) while any assignment is `active`/`paused` with a `next_billing_date` — the nightly run skips a card-less assignment *silently* and #779 does not exist yet — while replacing is never blocked. Member app: a Payment method section on My Membership; the hosted page renders the verification with no amount and a "save card" button, and `purpose=card_update` on the return URL is how the payment return page knows what came back), #791 (Payments documentation — one reliable source for the two processes. New `docs/payments.md`, written from the code on `main` and structured as the two flows the system actually runs: the member's first payment (CIT — member + Clerk link, the three `status = 'active'` insert paths, the staff and member payment-request routes, the hosted page's single-use token, and every branch of the Monei webhook) and the nightly recurring charge (MIT — the workflow and its secret, #780's calendar run guard, the selection query and what it does *not* check, date-aware pricing through the one `resolveMembershipFee()`, all five outcomes in a table, #785's dunning rule, #640's Retry / Manual payment, and cleanup). Plus reference sections: the five tables with their current CHECK sets and the migration holding each, where the four non-persisted projections live, the Assigned Plan status model and which statuses are unreachable (#786), the provider boundary and its env vars, the money rules linked rather than copied, what observability exists and what does not, a step-by-step **manual test runbook** against Monei test keys, and an Open gaps table naming #778–#790 so the document says what is not built. Alignment: the six payments rows of `docs/architecture.md`'s routers table and its `payments/` tree now keep a one-line contract and link to the process document, its TL;DR gains a Payments line, and the `001_ … 052_` migration range was stale; `docs/decisions.md` records the three settled choices of 2026-09-26; `docs/go-to-production.md` §5's items carry their ticket and point at the runbook. `PAYMENT_ENV` and `MONEI_ACCOUNT_ID` were read by the code but missing from `api/.env.example`. Docs only — no API, schema or UI change), #797 (Members — the expanded card shows the complete Member Profile, read-only: a new `PROFILE` section opens the expanded row with every field the Edit form submits plus the Member's Assigned Centers and Default Center, and it carries no input, no toggle and no Edit affordance — `⋮ → Edit` stays the only way in. One definition drives both sides (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/members/memberProfile.ts`: the field set, their read-only labels, the persisted-row → form-values mapping and the date formatting), so the read-only view and the form cannot drift apart, and the Profile renders the same list row the form is seeded from rather than re-reading the Member. The one read it adds is `GET /members/:memberId/centers`, which now answers the sole-active-center fallback itself (`soleActiveCenterId()`) instead of leaving each caller to guess that an empty `member_centers` list means "no center" — and 404s for a member outside the caller's gym, so that fallback cannot leak the caller's own center), #798 (Staff: expanding a card is a strictly read-only view of the complete Staff record — Personal / Contact / Employment / Schedule / Notes, Assigned Centers + Default Center, App access state — and the inline form, with its invite/resend/revoke actions, moved behind a new `⋮ → Edit` item. `staff/staffProfile.ts` declares the field set, its labels, its read-only formatting and the row → form mapping once, so the two halves cannot drift; no new endpoint, no API or schema change), #799 (Nutrition Library — read-only expanded view, Details modal, separate Edit flow: expanding either library's row now reads the complete food (Name → Description → Status → Translations → Categories → Nutritional Qualities → Media, the image last and read-only, the whole category and quality catalogue shown with the assigned entries in the Edit form's selected blue as spans rather than checkboxes), `⋮ → Details` opens a read-only modal with the name, the description and the full audit information plus the shared View Audit Log deep link, and `⋮ → Edit` is the only way anything changes — the Base library's Upload Image button moved from the expanded card into that form. Migration 196 adds what the row could not answer: `description VARCHAR(1000)` persisted across both routers' create/read/update, and the snapshot-at-write-time actor pairs `created_by_*`/`modified_by_*`/`deleted_by_*` plus `deleted_at`, each type under a CHECK — a superadmin administering a base food has no `gym_memberships` row to join to, the same reason `tax_rates.created_by_name` exists. One declaration drives both libraries (`apps/admin/src/components/nutritionLibrary/`: the row shape, the persisted-row → form-values mapping, the section order, the formatters, the chip style, the read-only view and the Details modal, whose Audit Log scope is the page's to pass), and no detail endpoint was added — the modal renders the list row), #800 (Centers: `⋮ → Edit` expands the Center row into an **inline** form instead of opening the Edit Center modal, which is gone — Name / Email / Phone / Address / Status / Theme, with Cancel and Save Changes inside the expanded row. Expanding still only reads, per #797/#798, and `centers/centerProfile.ts` declares the field set, its labels, its read-only formatting, the row → form mapping and the `PUT /centers/:id` payload once; no new endpoint, no API or schema change), #801 (Activities removed from Spaces: the Space form's `ACTIVITIES` checkbox list, the `GET`/`PUT /spaces/:id/activity-types` routes it drove, the assignment copy in `POST /spaces/:id/duplicate` and the `space_activity_types` join table are all gone — migration 197 drops it, and the relation that survives is the Activity Type's own `default_space_id`, edited on the Activity Types page and untouched here. No replacement Space-side relation, no Activity-entity change, and the Space's own fields, validation, permissions and `PUT /spaces/:id` payload are unchanged), #802 (Professional Services context menu: **Duplicate** first, the **Deactivate**·Activate slot second with Deactivate carrying `ContextMenu`'s shared `danger` style, **Details** always last — Edit and Delete keep their behaviour in the slots between, and a system row (which hides both) renders exactly Duplicate/Deactivate/Details. Presentation only: same menu component, same handlers, same `canWrite` gates, same endpoints, same Details modal; no API, schema or locale change), #803 (Exercises: the header button that opens the Import Exercises modal is labelled **Import System Exercises** — its own locale key `exercises.import_system_exercises`, because the modal's primary action keeps reading `Import`; and the modal's Cancel, which read as disabled at `#aaa` behind white text, takes the `#444` of ExerciseDetailModal's own Cancel and now dims only while an import is in flight. Label and styling only: the modal title, the import flow, its API, validation, permissions and the Add Exercise flow are unchanged), #804 (Exercises: **import is the visibility boundary** — `GET /exercises` returns the gym's own rows (`gym_id = ?`) and no longer `OR e.gym_id IS NULL`, so a Base Exercise appears on the Exercises page only once it has been imported, as the gym's copy carrying `cloned_from_id` (still badged **System sourced**). No new state: the copy *is* the import state (#718), so no column, migration or flag was added. Filtering in the list query rather than per-control is what keeps `?q=`, `?status=` and ordering from reaching a non-imported row. `GET /exercises/base` (the Import modal), `GET /exercises/:id`, `POST /exercises/import` and its duplicate protection are untouched, and the write paths already agreed with the new rule — `workout-templates.ts` and `training-plans.ts` validate an `exercise_id` with `gym_id = ?`, so the base rows the exercise pickers used to offer were rejected on save), #786 (retired the Assigned Plan's unreachable `draft`/`awaiting_payment` statuses, the Submit action and `POST /user-memberships/:id/submit` — an assignment is `active` from creation and its first payment is collected afterwards; migration 198 narrows the status CHECK and refuses to run while a row holds a retired status), #854 (removed the `draft` Billing Events projection #786 left without callers — `projectDraftBillingEvents()`, `computeMembershipFeePriceAt()`, the admin's Projected tag and its `billing_event_projected` label, and the always-false `projected` field of the Billing Events response (view and events); three `resolveMembershipFee()` rules only those tests pinned moved to `membership-fee-resolution.test.ts`), #779 (staff alert for failed payments awaiting action: `GET /payments/billing-events/attention` → `{ count, oldest_created_at }` over the Billing Events whose derived status is `failed` (`isAwaitingAction()`), a red count badge on Payments / Billing Events in the sidebar polled every 60 s, and a Payments Dashboard card; both open `billing-events?status=failed&order=asc`. Counts events, recurring charges only, no extra role gate beyond PAYMENTS read — the thread's proposed answers), #783 (Internal run routes: the nginx GitHub Actions IP allowlist on `/billing/` is removed rather than automated — `corback.conf` loses `location /billing/`, the allowlist file and its refresh script are deleted; a per-route `internalRunLimiter` in `app.ts` (`domain/internalRunRateLimit.ts`, `INTERNAL_RUN_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`/`_WINDOW_MINUTES`, default 10 per 15 min per IP, only 401s count) now fronts `/billing/*` and `/recurring-bookings/*`; installing the conf on the server and rotating both internal secrets stay in `go-to-production.md`), #790 (First payment on a back-dated assignment: the payment webhook stamps the first `next_billing_date` as the first `starts_at`-anchored cycle boundary strictly after UTC today, and any transition back to `active` walks a past `next_billing_date` forward the same way — `firstBillingDateAfter()` in `domain/billingDate.ts`, `domain/nextBillingDateStamp.ts`. Elapsed back-dated cycles and cycles missed while paused are written off (decisions (a1), no confirm flag, a pause is not a debt — `docs/payments.md` A5). No migration), #805 (Exercises: **+ Add Exercise** opens an inline creation card at the top of the list — the `CrudModal` is gone, and Cancel discards the staged form without calling the API. `exercises/exerciseForm.ts` declares the section order (**General** with Name / Description / Status, Configuration, Allowed Result Types, Muscles, **Media last**), the row → form mapping and both payloads once, so the creation card and the `⋮ → Edit` editor render one form body; the creation card is still the only half that submits `video_url` (#717 Q6 keeps it out of the `PUT`). Allowed Result Types is a reflowing checkbox grid reading `exercises.result_type_<slug>` labels (added for migration 073's nine slugs in en/es/ca, falling back to the catalogue's own `name` for a slug added later) instead of showing the raw key, and Media renders Image and Video side by side, stacking on a narrow card. Presentation only: same `POST`/`PUT /exercises`, same staged media uploads, same validation, permissions, import, duplicate, clone and status toggle; no API or schema change), #806 (Unify Exercise and Base Exercise editing: one editor, `components/exercises/ExerciseEditor.tsx`, rendered by both the gym Exercises page and the platform Base Exercises page, with `useExerciseEditorState()` beside it owning the form values, muscle roles, selected result types, validation, error line and saving flag, and `exerciseForm.ts` moved up out of `app/[locale]/exercises/` to sit with them. The Base Exercises editor gained the whole field set it never had — Status, the five Configuration defaults, Allowed Result Types, Muscles and Media — and its media upload/removal controls moved off the read-only expanded card into the editor behind `⋮ → Edit` (#797's rule); `ExerciseImageField`/`ExerciseVideoField` take the route root from the context (`basePath`, defaulting to `/exercises`) plus `requiresGymStorage`, since a Base Exercise's objects live in `cordel/Exercises/…` and no gym's bucket gates them. Removing media now asks first on **both** screens (#717 §7 generalised). New `GET /platform/exercises/lookups` (superadmin, registered before `/:id`) serves the muscle and result-type catalogues, because the gym-facing `/muscles` + `/result-types` sit behind a gym's module access and feature flags. No schema change, no endpoint merged: each page keeps its own routes and permissions), #808 (Language selector styling: the native `<select>` in the top header now gives every `<option>` the header's own colour pair, so the dropdown reads as a continuation of the band instead of the UA's white popup under the header's white text. The pair lives in the new `apps/admin/src/components/headerChrome.ts` — `HEADER_BG` / `HEADER_TEXT`, the exact token chains `TopHeader` paints with, plus `headerOptionStyle` — so a header control never restates them. Presentation only: the trigger, the three languages, the locale routing and every other header control are untouched; `GymSelector`/`CenterSelector` keep their pre-token `--chrome`/`#fff` options per the ticket's §6), #810 (Assigned Nutrition Plans: the sidebar entry and the page heading are both **Assigned Nutrition Plans** — one wording, via `nav.nutrition_plans` and `nutrition_plans.title`, which keep their keys — and the collapsed card header now carries the assignment plus the record's standard metadata: the assigned member reads at the plan name's own size and weight (one `headerTitleStyle` both cells spread), and `Created By` joins the `Created At` and the shared `StatusBadge` that were already there, rendered as `created_by_name ?? '—'` like every other list. Frontend only — `GET /member-nutrition-plans` already returned `created_by_name`, so no API, schema or behaviour change: creation, assignment, inline editing, the ⋮ menu, expansion and the member filter are untouched (§6)), #812 (Food Type selector fixed: the Add/Edit food form showed `nutrition_plan_templates.tree_component_type_undefined`, because `NutritionPlanTree` still read `nutrition_library_items.category` — the scalar column migration 142 (#501) replaced with the `nutrition_library_categories` catalogue and an M2M junction, so every food's type was `undefined`. It now reads the `categories: [{id, slug}]` the API has returned all along, and the options come from the backend: the accepted `component_type` set lives once in `api/src/domain/nutritionComponentTypes.ts` (the three routers' duplicated `COMPONENT_TYPES` consts are gone) and is served by `GET /nutrition-plan-templates|/member-nutrition-plans|/platform/nutrition-plan-templates/component-types`, so the selector can never offer a value the CHECK refuses — an assigned plan's meal items take four types (`chk_mnpmi_comp`, migration 105) where a template's take seven (`chk_nptmi_component_type`, migration 111, #294). Reopening an item now shows the Food Type it was saved with rather than the food's own first category, and every label goes through one `foodTypeLabel()` helper: the `{ defaultValue }` that was supposed to catch this does nothing, since next-intl's `t()` has no such option and prints a missing key verbatim. Frontend + one read endpoint per router; no migration, and no translation was added for `undefined`), #813 (Dietary Restrictions item picker shows the food's name alone: the parenthetical after each option is gone. It carried the food's own Food Type — empty `()` for every food until #812, since it read the scalar `category` migration 142 dropped, and the type once #812 fixed the read. A restriction picker is choosing a food to forbid, not classifying it, so the suffix is noise either way. One JSX expression in `NutritionPlanTree`'s restrictions section, shared by the gym and Base template pages and the Assigned Nutrition Plans page; the option's value, the POST to `/restrictions` and the configured-restriction rows are untouched), #814 (Membership Fee Promotions lose **Quantity**, **Every** and **Unit**: the section now edits Duration, Action, Value and Enabled — the four fields `resolveMembershipFee()` actually reads. The three came from the `promotion_period_benefits` shape the editor was built on ("2 sessions every 3 months") and said nothing about a membership fee, whose cadence is the Assigned Plan's own Billing frequency (`ASSIGNMENT_CADENCE`), never a Promotion's; nothing ever priced on them. Migration 199 drops the columns from `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` plus `chk_pmfb_positive` and `chk_pmfb_frequency_unit` (`chk_pmfb_action` stays), the singleton `PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit` stops requiring and persisting them, `POST /promotions/:id/duplicate` stops copying them and `fetchLiveBenefits()` stops freezing them onto the application snapshot. No assignment re-prices: the surviving columns are untouched and snapshots written before #814 keep their old keys, which no caller reads. Deploy the API before the migration, as for 176/177/179/197), #815 (Promotion sections renamed after the Promotion: **Session Promotion**, **One-off Promotion**, **Periodical Promotion**, **Membership Fee Promotion**, in the Promotion editor and in the applied-Promotion card of an Assigned Plan, with their empty states and `+ Add …` buttons. Label-only — the translation keys, the `session-benefits`/`oneoff-benefits`/`periodical-benefits` endpoint slugs, the `action` enum values, the snapshot and every pricing rule are untouched. A Membership Plan's own benefit sections keep the Plan's terminology (#816 requires it in as many words), and so do the Assigned Plan's own benefit sections, which render the assignment's snapshot of the Plan — which is why the promotion card got its own `promo_benefits_*` keys instead of sharing `AssignedPlanConfiguration`'s), #816 (Membership Plan expanded card is read-only, and PRICING moves up: expanding a Plan now renders the complete plan — GENERAL, PRICING, BILLING & DURATION, ONE-OFF BENEFITS, SESSION BENEFITS, PERIOD BENEFITS, CENTERS, BILLING EVENTS FORECAST, then PRICE HISTORY — with no section-level `Edit` button and no control that writes, and `⋮ → Edit` is the single entry point into the General form *and* into the five section-level editors, which are now gated on `isEditing` inside the one expanded body rather than living in the view half. Two defects that shape fixed: PRICING and PRICE HISTORY were invisible to anyone editing (Edit mode replaced the whole card with the General form, which is also why §4 reads as "currently only visible in the editing view"), and "Apply new price to assigned plans" — which repriced every Assigned Plan — sat in the read-only card. The Plan's own General columns and the section order are declared once in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/plans/planProfile.ts` (`PLAN_GENERAL_SECTION`, `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER`, `toPlanGeneralFormValues()`, `toPlanGeneralUpdatePayload()`), with `interface Plan extends PlanGeneralRow`. Presentation only: no migration, no API change, and each section still submits exactly what it did — the `plans.section_status` header becomes `plans.section_general` and the Plan's benefit sections keep the Plan's terminology (§14), never the Promotion names #815 introduced), #817 (Price History collapses, Current price shows gross + net: the PRICE HISTORY section of the expanded Membership Plan card is now a collapsible card that starts **collapsed** every time the plan is expanded (`CollapsibleSectionHeader` in `plans/page.tsx` — the whole header is a `<button>` carrying `aria-expanded`, the treatment #632 gave the Theme Colors groups; `priceHistoryOpen` is a per-plan id set that nothing seeds, and `toggleExpand` drops the id so re-expanding starts collapsed again). Its rows, dates, amounts and status badges are untouched. `Current price` reads as the customer price *and* its net — `€60.00 VAT included (net €49.59 + tax = €60.00)` — through `formatPlanCurrentPrice()` in `planProfile.ts`, which prints the server's own `amount_incl_tax`/`amount_excl_tax` and does no arithmetic of its own, so the card cannot drift from the Pricing editor's live preview. That exposed a real defect behind the row: a Plan on the **Default** tax rate (`membership_plans.tax_rate_id IS NULL`) had no rate for `computePriceFields()` to split at, so `enrichPlan` returned nulls and the Current price rendered `—` for a Plan that has a price — and the apply-to-assigned confirmation quoted an empty amount. `api/src/domain/planTaxRate.ts` (`selectPlanTaxRates()`) now decides that once, server-side: the Plan's **own** rate is what the Tax rate row displays (`null` ⇒ "Default", unchanged since #413), the **effective** rate is the Plan's own else the gym's system rate (`is_system = 1`, non-deleted — the row `seedSystemPtPackage()` already picks), and that is what the split and `applied_tax_rate` come from. One query serves both, so the list endpoint's per-plan round trips do not grow. No migration, no new endpoint, no change to any stored price. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/plans-price-history-collapsible.test.ts` (12), `api/src/test/plan-tax-rate.unit.test.ts` (8) and a new describe block in `membership-plans.test.ts` (system-rate fallback, own rate wins, no price ⇒ null split))

- **Done**: #647 (Book Personal Training Class Slots — all four stages of the plan agreed on the issue thread). **Stage 1 done**: migration 168 adds a nullable `professional_service_id` (FK→`professional_services` `ON DELETE SET NULL`) to both `activity_types` and `calendar_events`, so an occurrence can say which Professional Service (#484) delivers it; the Activity Type is the default and `activity-types.ts` propagates a change to not-yet-started occurrences through the existing `PROPAGATABLE_FIELDS` / `confirm_propagate` flow, `scheduleEngine.materializeScheduleRule()` stamps it on every generated occurrence, and both calendar routers accept it on create/update (a session inherits the Activity Type's value unless the field is sent). New `domain/professionalServices.ts` validates the id against the services visible **and enabled** for the gym (`gym_professional_services.status = 'active'`). New `domain/memberProfessionalServices.ts` answers the Member side — how many sessions the Member holds per Professional Service, summed across purchased `user_class_packages`, `promotion_session` benefits on applied promotions of active assignments, and `user_membership_services` attachments of Session-type items, all resolved through `sellable_item_professional_services` (#546) — served read-only by `GET /members/:memberId/professional-services`. No UI and no booking behaviour change yet. **Stage 2 done**: no migration — `GET /members/:memberId/personal-training-slots` (`api/member-personal-training-slots.ts`) projects the next 2 months of `calendar_events` whose `professional_service_id` the Member holds into recurring Mon–Sun slots, grouped by *gym-local* weekday + start/end time + activity type + professional service (local, not UTC, so a slot survives a DST change). Each slot carries one entry per expected date with a status (`available` / `no_occurrence` / `not_scheduled` / `full` / `already_booked`) plus `available_count` and `fully_available`: per the thread's Q5 answer a taken or unscheduled date is reported, never a reason to drop the weekday from the grid. The grouping rules are pure in `domain/personalTrainingSlots.ts`; #481 eligibility is asked through the new exported `isActivityTypeEligibleForMember()` that the booking hook itself now calls, so the projection can't drift from `bookMemberOnSession`. Admin: display-only `Personal Training Class Slots` section in the expanded Member row (`members/MemberPersonalTrainingSlots.tsx`). **Stage 3 done**: migration 169 adds `member_recurring_slots` — the Member's *weekly pattern* (`iso_weekday` 1=Mon…7=Sun, gym-local `start_time`/`end_time`, activity type, professional service, nullable center), unique through a VIRTUAL `selection_key` that folds a NULL center to 0; `PUT /members/:memberId/personal-training-slots/selections` replaces the whole set (every identity must be a slot the projection currently offers) and `POST /members/:memberId/personal-training-slots/book` books the window for it, optionally replacing the set in the same call. Both are `requireModuleWrite('MEMBERS')`. Booking goes one occurrence at a time through `bookMemberOnSession` in its own transaction, so capacity, waitlist mode, #481 eligibility and package-credit debiting stay the booking path's business and one rejected date cannot roll back the rest; a date that filled up since the projection is rolled back out of the waitlist and reported as `full` (§3: never book a slot that became unavailable), and the report names every date it skipped or failed. A stored slot the grid no longer offers books nothing (§5) and existing bookings are never touched by deselecting. Admin: the same section is now selectable (checkbox per slot, Save selection / Book, per-date result list). **Stage 4 done**: migration 170 adds `recurring_booking_run_log` (a `billing_run_log`-shaped singleton) and extends `chk_member_notifications_type`; `POST /recurring-bookings/run` (`api/recurring-bookings.ts`, `X-Internal-Secret`, fired by `.github/workflows/recurring-booking-run.yml` at 03:00 UTC) maintains the rolling 2-month window. Per the thread's Q4 answer it is an endpoint an external scheduler calls, not an in-process timer — the `POST /billing/run` precedent, and the only shape that behaves when the API runs more than one replica. Each run re-projects every member with stored selections from *now* and books the free dates through the very same `bookSelectedSlots()` the Book button runs (extracted from the stage-3 route, so capacity, waitlist mode, #481 eligibility, credit debiting and the §5 service re-check exist once); the window advances because "now" moved, with no per-member bookkeeping that could drift, and §4's "creates only missing bookings, never duplicates" falls out of the projection. Dates it cannot take raise a `recurring_booking_skipped` member notification (the thread's Q5 answer — festivity, gym closed, or taken by another booking), deduped on (slot, date) over a 120-day lookback so a rolling window does not re-alert nightly; `already_booked` and internal failures never notify. An unscoped run takes the 23-hour lock; a run scoped to a `gym_id`/`member_id` skips it so an operator can re-run one gym after a fix. The job honours the `organization.professional_services` flag, and one member's failure is caught and reported without ending the night's run.

- **Done (continued)**: #677 (Fix Theme Card Border and Border Radius Settings: the Theme editors' **Cards** group — `cardBorder` (a colour) and `cardBorderRadius` (an `advanced` attribute) — now actually paints the cards, in both Base and Custom Themes. Two defects: `cardBorderRadius` was editable and persisted but never written to any CSS variable, so the Card Border Radius did nothing anywhere; and most card surfaces restated a border colour (`#e2e2e6`, `#ececf0`, `#e0e0e8`) and a `borderRadius` of their own, so even `--gd-card-border` — which `applyTokens()` has always written — only reached the handful of pages that used the variable. `applyTokens()` now emits `--gd-card-radius` through an exported `CARD_ADVANCED_VARS` map, mirroring `CALENDAR_ADVANCED_VARS` and falling back to `DEFAULT_ADVANCED` for a theme with no `advanced` map or a blank value (the calendar's length-fallback logic extracted as the shared `cssLengthValue()`); and every card surface in the admin app — 21 `cardStyle`/`cardSt` definitions plus the inline list cards on Themes, Staff and Centers — spreads one shared `cardSurfaceStyle` (`apps/admin/src/components/ui.tsx`) carrying `border` + `borderRadius` + `background` as `var()`s with their default token values as CSS fallbacks. A highlighted or being-edited card overrides only `border` on top of the spread and keeps the themed radius. Base ↔ Custom is unchanged: both are applied through the same `applyTokens()`, so a Base Theme's values paint when it is the applied theme and a Custom Theme's override when it is (there is still no live sparse inheritance — cloning copies the blob, as since #559). Frontend-only: no migration, no API change, no i18n change, and no other theme setting touched. One deliberate visual change: cards that hardcoded `borderRadius: 10` now follow the token, whose default is `8px`. New `apps/admin/src/test/theme-card-css.test.ts` (7 cases: the attribute → variable map, emission for a configured theme, for a legacy theme with no `advanced` map and for a blank value, the configured-over-default precedence, the Cards colour group's fields, the shared style's variables and its fallback matching the default token, and a source scan asserting no card style declares a border radius or a border colour of its own))

- **Done (continued)**: #678 (Unify Base Theme Editor with Custom Theme Editor: expanding a Base Theme row in Cordel → Base Themes (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/system/themes/page.tsx`) now opens the same structured editor the Custom Themes screen uses — collapsible **Branding / Colors / Typography** sections over a Save/Cancel footer — instead of a read-only grid of colour swatches that exposed nothing but colours and hid the rest behind a separate *Edit* step. `Assignments` is the one Custom section with no platform counterpart (a theme is assigned to a *gym's* centres). The section wrapper and the Branding block (name, description, logo upload/removal, `logo_contains_gym_name`) moved into a shared `apps/admin/src/components/ThemeSectionEditor.tsx` (`ThemeSection`, `ThemeBrandingEditor`), joining the Colors/Typography editors already shared through `ThemeTokensEditor.tsx`, so a future theme setting is implemented once. On the Custom Themes screen a Base Theme now shows all four sections instead of Assignments + Colours, read-only: read-only means disabled controls, never a different set of controls, so `ThemeAdvancedSection` renders disabled rather than being omitted and `ThemeTypographyEditor` gained the `readOnly` prop `ThemeColorsEditor` already had. No change to the theme engine, persistence, status workflow or Base↔Custom inheritance — same `PUT /platform/themes/:id`, same status select, same #492 draft/live-preview/dirty-guard. Frontend-only: no migration, no API change; `themes.label_description` added to `es.json`/`ca.json` where it was missing and rendered as its raw key. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/theme-editor-unification.test.ts` (9 cases))
- **Done (continued)**: #694 (Website registration no longer fails for a gym with one active and one inactive center: every sole-center fallback now goes through `soleActiveCenterId()` (`api/src/infra/centerContext.ts`) and counts active centers only; for website registration an inactive center behaves exactly like a deleted one (never counts towards "more than one center", explicit inactive `center_id` → same 400 as an unknown id; the self-registration first sign-in falls back to the first active center); the missing-center error now reads `center_id is required as the gym has more than one center`. Staff-side explicit center choices unchanged. No migration.)
- **Done (continued)**: #696 (Member app `/link`: setting a password after an invitation no longer fails with "Something went wrong". The password step called `signUp.password({ password, emailAddress })`, and re-sending `emailAddress` un-verified the address the invitation ticket had verified; it now calls `signUp.create({ strategy: 'ticket', ticket, password })`, keeping the ticket-verified email. No API change.)
- **Done (continued)**: #698 (Docs: the invitation email is Clerk's single *Invitation* template per instance, with no per-invitation language in Clerk's API — the language is chosen inside the template, see #701.
