Documented in `docs/wordpress-integration.md`; `docs/go-to-production.md` gains an item to recreate the customised template on the Clerk production instance (a premium feature there). No code change.)
- **Done (continued)**: #701 (Invitation language: website registration defaults `locale` to `es`, and copies `ca`/`en` into the invitation's `publicMetadata.lang` so the Clerk *Invitation* template — which picks Catalan/English from `lang` and falls back to Spanish via Handlebars `#if` — and the `/{locale}/link` page always match. `lang` is cleared with `gym_signup` on first sign-in. No migration.)
- **Done (continued)**: #702 (Member app `/link`: after a successful `POST /me/link` the page does a full navigation to Home (`window.location.replace`) instead of `router.replace`, so `AppContext` — which loaded `/me/gyms` and `/me/profile` on mount, before the member existed — loads again and the greeting shows the member's name straight after sign-up. No API change.)
- **Done (continued)**: #704 (Admin Members list: **Email** column after Name, so website sign-ups — identified by name + email — can be told apart without expanding rows. Frontend only: `GET /members` already returned `email` and search already matched it; new i18n key `members.col_email` en/es/ca.)
- **Done (continued)**: #707 (Admin Members list: the Email column no longer wraps one character per line — `word-break: break-all` let `DataTable`'s auto layout squeeze it to ~85px even at 1440px. Plain text like System → Users, with a `<wbr>` after `@` so narrow screens split it in two at most.)
- **Done (continued)**: #709 (Orphaned Clerk accounts: one shared definition of a *linked* login — superadmin, staff `gym_memberships`, or an active member — in `infra/clerk-account-links.ts`. (1) `DELETE /members/:id` deletes the Clerk user first when it was its last link and always removes this gym's member-role `gym_memberships` row; (2) the Clerk `user.deleted` webhook unlinks every Gymdesk row; (3) superadmin *Cordel → Orphaned accounts* lists unlinked logins with a reason and deletes them (re-checked, Clerk first, audited). Members page Delete/Revoke moved from `confirm()` to `ConfirmDialog`. No migration.)
- **Done (continued)**: #726 (Audit Log labels: en/es/ca translations for the `system` and `provider` sources and for the 25 audit actions the API writes without a label (e.g. `restore`, `activate`, `refund_credit`, `impersonation_started`); Source filter gains System/Provider; `AUDIT_ACTIONS` gains the 21 written-but-unlisted actions so they can be filtered.)
- **Done (continued)**: #729 (CI: the #709 audit-row tests poll with a new `eventually()` test helper instead of a fixed 50 ms sleep (fire-and-forget audit writes were slower on CI); Deploy API prunes dangling podman images before its free-disk check — deploys had been aborting on a full VPS disk since #721.)
- **Done (continued)**: #733 (Docs: `architecture.md` release steps mention the API deploy's dangling-image prune and 1 GB free check (#729); the CI/CD secrets table lists `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` — must equal the Clerk endpoint's signing secret, endpoint enabled and subscribed to `user.created` + `user.deleted`.)
- **Done (continued)**: #676 (Activities list: the colour bullet leaves the name cell and becomes a **Colour** column of its own — swatch + hex — between a new truncated **Description** column and *Created by*, the column order the Plans page uses. An Activity with no colour shows `—`, never a fallback swatch; the editable Colour field in the inline edit form and the add row is untouched. Frontend only; new i18n keys `activity_types.col_description` / `col_color` en/es/ca.)

- **Done (continued)**: #712 (Custom Themes header shows who created a Theme, when, and whether it is the gym's Theme: migration 178 adds the `created_by_name`/`created_by_type` actor snapshot to `themes` (the same immutable-snapshot shape as `themes.deleted_by_name` and `tax_rates.created_by_name`), backfilled from the earliest `create`/`clone` `audit_logs` entry per theme; `POST /system/themes/clone/:sourceId` — the only path that creates a Customer Theme — captures it, and every `/system/themes` response gains `created_by_name` plus `is_gym_theme`, derived from `gyms.theme_id` for the *requesting* gym. The Customer Themes card header renders Created by · Created at for Custom Themes and a ★ Gym theme badge on the assigned one; Base Theme rows and the editor sections are unchanged.), #713 (Custom Theme logos move out of the `themes.logo_bytes` MEDIUMBLOB into the gym's Cloudflare R2 folder: `POST /system/themes/:id/logo` uploads to `<gyms.storage_folder_prefix>/Branding/Logo/logo.<ext>` — extension from the validated MIME type, never from the file name — and migration 180 adds `themes.logo_object_key` plus `CHECK chk_themes_logo_storage`; the public URL is derived at read time and exposed as `logo_url` on every theme-shaped response (`/system/themes`, `/gyms`, `/me/gym`, `/me/gyms`), which both app headers and the Custom Themes page now prefer, with `GET /themes/:id/logo` still serving either storage mode. The key names the gym, so an upload hands the branding slot over to the uploading theme and deletes the object it replaces. Base Theme logos and Custom logos uploaded before #713 stay blob-backed until replaced; `logo_contains_gym_name` still decides whether the gym name renders next to the logo.), #725 (Custom Themes configure the six **Members App background images** — My Training Plan, My Nutrition, Calendar, My Bookings, My Membership and the general background: migration 181 adds `theme_member_images` (one row per configured `(theme_id, slot)`, carrying the R2 object key and `gym_id`), `POST`/`DELETE /system/themes/:id/members-images/:slot` upload and clear a slot, and every theme-shaped response gains `members_images` — six fields, `null` for an unconfigured slot, resolved to URLs by the new `domain/themeMemberImages.ts` with the `?v=` stamp the logo already uses. The object key is derived server-side from the tenant's own `storage_folder_prefix`, the theme row and the slot — `<prefix>/Themes/<theme_id>-<name>/Members/<slot>.png` — so the uploaded file name never reaches it and a gym cannot aim an upload anywhere but its own folder; the missing folder markers are written first, idempotently. Uploads are PNG/JPEG/WebP ≤ 4 MB, validated by signature rather than by the `Content-Type` header, and a refused upload leaves the existing image and its reference untouched. **Remove clears the row only** — #725 requires the object to survive it — so a re-upload reuses the same deterministic key. In the Theme editor the new *Members App* section stages picks and removals in the draft and performs them on Save, the lifecycle the logo has had since #188; in the member app `components/MembersBackground.tsx` paints the slot the current route maps to onto `<body>` as centred `cover` artwork under a scrim of the theme's own `pageBackground`, and a `null` slot leaves the theme background colour. Base Themes are out of scope (six nulls) and a clone starts unconfigured, since cloning has never copied a theme's R2 assets.), #728 (the six Members App theme images become the **backgrounds of the Members home sections**: the Calendar / My Training Plan / My Bookings / My Nutrition tiles and the My Membership card each paint their own slot, over the general `background` slot #725 already paints on the page. Frontend-only — no migration, no API change, no request per section: the URLs are already on the `theme.members_images` payload the app holds. New `apps/member/src/components/MembersSectionCard.tsx` renders the element the page already rendered (a tile `<button>`, the membership `<div role="button">`) with the caller's own styles and replaces only its `background`, so the grid, the card dimensions, the icons, the labels and the navigation are untouched; a card's artwork is `scroll` rather than the page's `fixed` (a viewport-anchored image would show a different crop in every card) under a scrim of the theme's own `cardBackground` at 82 %, heavier than the page's 72 % because the text sits directly on it. A `null` slot leaves the card exactly as it is today — the member app still resolves no fallback of its own. The Next Booking and Alerts cards deliberately keep their plain surfaces. 17 structural/unit cases in the member app's `members-section-backgrounds.test.ts`.), #732 (**Base Themes configure the same six Members App background images**: migration 182 widens `theme_member_images.gym_id` to nullable so a row can belong to the platform, and a Base Theme's images live in the platform's own folder — `cordel/Themes/<theme_id>-<sanitized theme name>/Members/<slot>.png`, built from the new `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (`cordel`, the sibling of #668's `gyms/` root) by the *same* `buildThemeMemberImageKey()`. `POST`/`DELETE /platform/themes/:id/members-images/:slot` (superadmin) mirror the gym routes of #725 — signature-validated PNG/JPEG/WebP ≤ 4 MB, the four folder markers written first, the deterministic key derived server-side, Remove clearing the row and leaving the object — and resolve the theme with `gym_id IS NULL`, so each router 404s on the other's themes. Every `/platform/themes` payload carries `members_images`; on the gym side `shapeTheme()` stops forcing a Base Theme to six nulls and `loadMemberImagesByTheme()` includes `gym_id IS NULL` rows for theme ids the caller already named, so `GET /system/themes` shows them read-only and `/me/gym(s)` — and therefore `MembersBackground`/`MembersSectionCard` — paint them with no member-app change at all. The Base Theme editor (Cordel → Themes) gains the same **Members App** section, reusing #725's `ThemeMembersImagesEditor` with its stage-until-Save draft. A clone still starts unconfigured. 25 integration cases in `base-theme-members-images.test.ts`, 4 unit cases for the platform keys, 8 structural cases in the admin app's `base-theme-members-images.test.ts`.), #735 (**Gym Bucket Initialization creates the gym-level `Themes/` folder**: `POST /platform/gyms/:id/storage/initialize` now writes one more zero-byte marker — `gyms/<gym_id>-<sanitized_gym_name>/Themes/` — appended to `GYM_FOLDERS` so every folder that existed before keeps the position it was written in. No migration and no new endpoint: the action already rewrites the whole marker set under the prefix captured on first init, so a gym provisioned before this ticket receives the folder by re-running it, and re-running stays idempotent (every key ends in `/`, so the object a marker overwrites is always another marker, never a file). The root only — a Custom Theme's `Themes/<theme_id>-<name>/Members/` branch cannot exist before the theme does and stays with `ensureStorageFolders()` at upload time (#725). `'Themes'` now has one spelling, `THEMES_FOLDER` in `infra/storage.ts`, which `domain/themeMemberImages.ts` re-exports as its `THEME_STORAGE_FOLDER`. 4 unit cases added to `storage.test.ts` (the marker is written, it is the same folder a theme folder prefix is built from, nothing theme-specific is created below it, a second run writes the same keys as empty markers) and 1 integration case in `gyms-storage.test.ts` for re-initializing a gym provisioned before the folder existed.)
- **Done (continued)**: #720 (Workout exercise rows show the media the exercise already carries: every endpoint that returns a workout exercise — the Workout Template and Training Plan JSON trees, the Training Plan Template tree, the block-exercise list and the create/update/move/duplicate responses — now projects `exercise_image_url` and `exercise_video_url` straight off `exercises`, so no workout view looks media up per row. One shared `apps/admin/src/components/ExerciseMediaThumbnails.tsx` renders them at the right of the row in all four admin views (Workouts/Training Plan builder, Workout Templates tree, Training Plan Templates tree, member Training Plan block exercises): a lazy-loaded image thumbnail, and a video tile with a play indicator that links out — never a `<video>` element, so no MP4 is downloaded to draw a row. A YouTube video URL gets its poster from `img.youtube.com`; anything else falls back to a plain play tile. No schema change and no source/ownership logic in the workout layer — the 512×512 image thumbnail and the stored video poster arrive with #719, and the component prefers them as soon as the API returns them.)
- **Done (continued)**: #722 (Member app Nutrition foods are horizontal, swipeable image cards: `GET /me/nutrition-plan` now projects the Nutrition Library item's own `image_url` on every meal item and attaches its nutritional qualities (#644) — read once for the whole plan, not per item, so no third level of N+1 and no extra request per food. Two new Member components, `NutritionFoodCard` (square image with a placeholder fallback, name, quantity+unit, role, quality chips — every optional line omitted when absent) and `NutritionFoodCarousel` (one carousel per meal, the meal's foods being the alternatives a member chooses between). The swipe is the browser's own: a flex row with CSS scroll snapping, no gesture library and no `touchmove`/`preventDefault`, so vertical page scrolling is untouched; the buttons, dots and the `aria-live` counter all read the real scroll position. Images lazy-load except the first card. No schema change, no new endpoint, and the Nutrition data model is untouched.)
- **Done (continued)**: #723 (Members → My Training Plan shows each exercise's image and video, inside the exercise's own row: no API or schema change was needed — `GET /me/training-plans` reads the same `PLAN_TREE_SELECT` that already projects `exercise_image_url`/`exercise_video_url` off `exercises` (#720), so the media arrives with the plan tree and costs no request per exercise. Three new Member files: `lib/exerciseMedia.ts` (pure helpers — thumbnail-over-master, YouTube id/poster/embed, and the video *kind* that decides how a URL can be played), `components/ExerciseMedia.tsx` (a lazy-loaded image tile and a video tile with a play indicator, rendered next to the exercise's sets and reps; nothing at all when the exercise has no media, and a broken thumbnail hides itself without touching the other) and `components/ExerciseMediaViewer.tsx` (the larger views — an overlay, never a route, so the member keeps their position, the selected weekday and their scroll: Escape and a click outside close it, the close button takes focus and hands it back to the tile). A YouTube video plays in a `youtube-nocookie` embed, an object in R2 in a `<video controls preload="metadata">`, and a URL the app cannot embed opens in a new tab; nothing autoplays and no `<video>` is mounted to draw a row. The hierarchy, ordering and weekday navigation of the plan are untouched, and the Member app resolves nothing about where the media came from — no System/Custom, Base/Gym, import or inheritance logic.)

- **Done (continued)**: #724 (Training Plans' filters and list header adopt the Assigned Plans pattern: the five filters (Name, Member, Source, Author, Status) move out of the page header into a labelled filter bar — each with its label above its control — and the naked row of small sort buttons becomes the list's own column-header band. Two shared pieces came out of Assigned Plans to make that reuse rather than a copy: `components/FilterBar.tsx` (`FilterBar`/`FilterField` + `filterControlStyle`, now used by both pages) and `components/listChrome.ts`, the surface/header band/cell insets/row dividers `DataTable` itself is built from, so a card list wears the same chrome without restating a grey or a padding. Header and rows share one `LIST_COLUMNS`-derived grid and one `overflow-x: auto` wrapper (#637's rule), so titles line up with their values and a narrow viewport scrolls the list, not the page. Presentation only: the same five filters with the same behaviour, the same sort keys, the same columns, the same values (member and description on a second line under the plan name, the end date under the start date) and the same expand/edit/context-menu behaviour; the page title and `+ New Training Plan` are untouched.)

- **Done (continued)**: #718 (Import Base Exercises into Gym Exercises: the gym Exercises page's **Import Defaults** button — which seeded eight hardcoded names — becomes **Import**, opening an *Import Exercises* modal over the platform Base Exercises library. Two new gym-facing endpoints: `GET /exercises/base?q=&muscle=` lists active library rows with their muscles and an `imported_exercise_id` marking the gym's own copy (matched on `cloned_from_id` *or* name, so pre-#718 copies aren't offered again), and `POST /exercises/import` copies `{ baseExerciseIds }` in one transaction, keeping each base name (no "(Copy)" suffix) and recording `cloned_from_id`. Ids that aren't active base exercises — including another gym's custom exercise — are a 400 with `invalid_ids`; ids the gym already has come back as `skipped`. The modal filters server-side, keeps its selection across filter changes, restricts *Select all matching* to the filtered importable rows, and shows already-imported rows disabled. Source labels in the list become **System sourced** (`gym_id IS NULL` or `cloned_from_id` set) and **Custom** — a label change only, the ownership model is untouched. `POST /exercises/import-defaults` and its `DEFAULT_EXERCISES` list are removed.)
- **Done (continued)**: #757 (Website registration joins the **gym** only: `center_id` removed from `POST /public/gyms/:gymRef/registrations` (one sent by an older site is dropped, not rejected), no center resolution or center-related 400s, `gym_signup` metadata is `{ gym_id, name }`; first sign-in (`/me/link`) creates the member with no `member_centers` row. Single-active-center gyms keep working through `centerContext`'s sole-center fallback; in multi-center gyms staff assign the center. No migration.)
- **Done (continued)**: #759 (Member app `/link`: an invitation link opened while a *different* account is signed in no longer links that account (404 → "Algo salió mal", invitation left unused). When the URL carries `__clerk_ticket` and someone is signed in, the page signs them out — `signOut` callback, so no Clerk redirect — and reloads the same URL, which redeems the ticket. No API change.)
- **Done (continued)**: #719 part 1 (Gym Exercise images are a 2048×2048 master plus a 512×512 thumbnail: migration 187 adds `exercises.image_thumbnail_url` next to `image_url` — no backfill, and a NULL thumbnail is simply "no thumbnail", never "ask the library". `POST /exercises/:id/image` is the only writer of the pair: the **browser** draws the thumbnail from the master (`apps/admin/src/lib/exerciseImageUpload.ts`, per the ticket's Q2 answer — no `sharp` or `ffmpeg` in the API image) and uploads both in one JSON request, and the server re-validates each file from its own bytes (PNG signature, exact square, alpha channel) rather than from the `Content-Type` header or the file name. Both objects land under the gym's own prefix at `<prefix>/Exercises/Images/<id>-<Name>[-thumbnail].png`, never in `cordel/`; nothing is uploaded and nothing is written until both files pass, so a refused upload leaves the existing image exactly as it was. Replacement and removal are ownership-aware: the old objects go only after the row points at the new ones, only when they sit under this gym's prefix (a System `cordel/…` object, another gym's and an external link are never deleted) and only when no other exercise still references them — `duplicate`/`clone`/`import` copy media *references*, so two rows can share one object. `DELETE /exercises/:id/image` clears both references and does **not** fall back to the Base Exercise's media; re-importing is the supported way back. Uploading or removing never changes **System sourced** to **Custom**. The Exercises page manages the image through a dedicated control rather than a form field (so cancelling an edit can neither undo an upload nor re-apply a removed image), the Add modal stages the pair and uploads it the moment the exercise exists, and every workout projection now also returns `exercise_image_thumbnail_url` — which #720/#723 already preferred — so no list row downloads a 2048px master. **Part 2 landed next**; part 3 (import/re-import media copying) landed after #741.)
- **Done (continued)**: #719 part 2 (A Gym Exercise video is an MP4 plus the 512×512 poster the UI draws instead of downloading it: migration 188 adds `exercises.video_thumbnail_url` next to `video_url` — no backfill, so a YouTube or hand-typed link simply has no stored poster and the apps keep deriving one. `POST /exercises/:id/video` is the only writer of the pair: the **browser** captures one frame of the clip into a square canvas (`apps/admin/src/lib/exerciseVideoUpload.ts`, the same #719 Q2 answer — no `sharp`/`ffmpeg` in the API image) and uploads both files in one JSON request, and the server validates each from its own bytes — the MP4's `ftyp` brand, `moov` and `stsd` video sample entries (`api/src/domain/mp4Video.ts`, no `ffprobe`), the poster's PNG signature and exact 512×512 — never from the `Content-Type` header or the file name, so a QuickTime `.mov` renamed to `.mp4` and an audio-only file are both refused. `EXERCISE_VIDEO_MAX_MB` (default 50) bounds the upload, which the route parses with its own `express.json` limit. Both objects land under the gym's own prefix at `<prefix>/Exercises/Videos/<id>-<Name>.mp4` and `…-thumbnail.png`, never in `cordel/`; nothing is uploaded and nothing is written until both files pass. Replacement and removal reuse part 1's ownership rules unchanged — the old objects go only after the row points at the new ones, only when they are this gym's own, and only when no other exercise still references them — and `DELETE /exercises/:id/video` clears both references without falling back to the Base Exercise's media. Uploading or removing never changes **System sourced** to **Custom**. The Exercises page manages the video through a dedicated control beside the image one, the Add modal stages the pair, and every workout projection now also returns `exercise_video_thumbnail_url`, which #720/#723 already preferred. **Part 3 landed next**: import/re-import media copying, once #741 had merged.)
- **Done (continued)**: #719 part 3 (Import and **re-import** carry the media references: `POST /exercises/import` already copied a Base Exercise's four media references into the gym's new copy (§2 — references only, no System object is duplicated into a gym's folder) and the copy owns them from then on (§3). What part 3 adds is the way *back*: §12 makes re-importing the only supported restore path — there is deliberately no "restore System media" action and no runtime fallback — so an id the gym already has no longer just reports `skipped`. Its copy's media references are refreshed from the Base Exercise's current ones and come back under `refreshed`, making the response `{ imported, refreshed, skipped }`. One pure rule decides it (`api/src/domain/exerciseMediaImport.ts`): the image pair and the video pair move independently, and a pair the Base Exercise does not have restores nothing — a base row with no image leaves the gym's own upload exactly where it is, because a routine re-import must not delete work the gym did. A refresh touches media only: the name, description, defaults and `cloned_from_id` of a copy the gym may have edited are its own, so a row matched by name alone keeps reading as **Custom**. Objects the copy stopped pointing at are swept after the transaction commits, through part 1's ownership check — a `cordel/…` System object, another gym's object, an external link and an object another exercise still references are never deleted — and each refresh is audited as an `update`. `GET /exercises/base` now carries all four references plus `media_refreshable`, the SQL mirror of the same rule, so the Import modal enables an already-imported row only when a re-import would move something, badges it **Media update**, and resolves nothing itself; *Select all matching* stays away from those rows. 12 unit cases for the rule, 23 integration cases in `exercise-media-reimport.test.ts` (which mocks R2), and 5 more structural cases in the admin app. No migration — #719's own columns already hold everything.)
- **Done (continued)**: #716 (Base Exercises carry the same image pair: a base exercise is a `gym_id IS NULL` row and belongs to no gym, so its master and thumbnail live in the platform's own R2 folder at `cordel/Exercises/Images/<id>-<Name>[-thumbnail].png` — `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (#732), never a gym prefix — built by the new `domain/baseExerciseImages.ts` off the same `Exercises/Images` leaf and the same name sanitizer the gym side uses. No migration: `exercises.image_url` + `image_thumbnail_url` (migration 187) hold the resulting Cloudflare URLs for a base row exactly as they do for a gym-owned one, and no object-key column was added (§7). `POST`/`DELETE /platform/exercises/:id/image` (superadmin) are the only writer and clearer; the pair is prepared in the browser by #719's own `lib/exerciseImageUpload.ts` and re-validated by #719's own `validateExerciseImagePair()` — #716 §2/§3 are #719 §5 word for word, so there is one implementation of "a valid exercise image", not two. Each router answers for its own rows: the platform route is 404 for a gym's exercise, the gym route 403 for a base one. Stale objects are swept with the mirror of the gym rule — `isPlatformOwnedExerciseImageUrl()` refuses a gym object and an external URL — plus a reference check that spans **every** tenant, because `POST /exercises/import` copies media *references* and each gym that imported the exercise points at the platform's own object; removal clears both references with no fallback. Cordel → Base Exercises becomes an expandable list like Base Nutrition Library: the image, `Upload Image`/`Replace`, `Remove` and `View full size` (the only thing that fetches the 2048px master) live on the expanded card, name/description edit **inline** and the `CrudModal`s are gone (the ticket's Q4 answer), and the card offers the shared View Audit Log deep link. **Generating the artwork is out of scope** (the thread's answer to Q1/Q2): no generator, no backfill script, so an existing base exercise stays image-less until an administrator uploads a master — tracked in `docs/go-to-production.md`. 35 integration cases in `base-exercise-images.test.ts`, 15 unit cases for the platform keys and ownership test, 12 structural cases in the admin app's `base-exercise-images.test.ts`.)
- **Done (continued)**: #717 (Base Exercises carry a demonstration **video** too: the image pair of #716 one folder over, at `cordel/Exercises/Videos/<id>-<Name>.mp4` and `…-thumbnail.png` — `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT`, never a gym prefix — built by the new `domain/baseExerciseVideos.ts` off the same `Exercises/Videos` leaf and the same name sanitizer the gym side uses. No migration: `exercises.video_url` + `video_thumbnail_url` (migration 188) hold the resulting Cloudflare URLs for a base row exactly as they do for a gym-owned one, and no `video_object_key` column was added (the ticket's Q5 answer). `POST`/`DELETE /platform/exercises/:id/video` (superadmin) are the only writer and clearer; the **browser** captures the 512×512 poster with #719's own `lib/exerciseVideoUpload.ts` and the server re-validates the pair with #719's own `validateExerciseVideoPair()` + `domain/mp4Video.ts` — Q3 and Q4 confirmed reusing both, so there is one implementation of "a valid exercise video" and no `ffprobe` in the API image. `EXERCISE_VIDEO_MAX_MB` (default 50, clamped at 200) and the buffered `PutObject` path are reused unchanged (Q2), with the platform route mounting its own `express.json` limit in `app.ts`. Each router answers for its own rows: the platform route is 404 for a gym's exercise, the gym route 403 for a base one. Stale objects are swept with `isPlatformOwnedExerciseVideoUrl()` (a gym object and an external YouTube link are never deleted by a platform operation) plus a reference check that spans **every** tenant and **all four** media columns, because `POST /exercises/import` copies media *references*; removal clears both references with no fallback, and a `PUT` that repoints `video_url` still drops the poster that belonged to the MP4. The expanded card on Cordel → Base Exercises gains a **Media** section holding the image block and the new video block: the stored poster with a play affordance, `Upload Video`/`Replace`, `Remove` behind the page's confirmation, and no `<video>` mounted until the player is asked for — then with `controls` and `preload="metadata"` and nothing else, because §8 forbids autoplay. Per the ticket's Q6 answer the gym Exercises card's media section is renamed **Media** and no longer offers `video_url` as an editable text box (the edit PUT stops sending it, so a video uploaded while the editor was open can no longer be repointed by saving the form); nothing else on either card is hidden or moved. Base videos are also what makes #719 part 3's re-import able to restore a System poster. 42 integration cases in `base-exercise-videos.test.ts`, 15 unit cases for the platform keys and ownership test, 12 structural cases in the admin app.)
- **Done (continued)**: #715 (Base Nutrition Library foods carry an image: a base food is a `gym_id IS NULL` row and belongs to no gym, so its image lives in the platform's own R2 folder at `cordel/Nutrition/<food_id>-<sanitized name>.png` — `PLATFORM_STORAGE_ROOT` (#732), never a gym prefix. No migration: the existing `nutrition_library_items.image_url` column (migration 138) holds the Cloudflare URL for a base food exactly as it already does for a gym-owned one, and the row's ownership decides the folder. `POST /platform/nutrition-library/:id/image` (superadmin) validates the bytes rather than the request — PNG signature, 512×512 from the IHDR, an alpha channel — writes the two folder markers, uploads, and only then updates the row, so a refused upload leaves the existing image untouched; a food renamed since its last upload has its old object swept best-effort afterwards. The expanded food card on Cordel → Base Nutrition Library shows the image over a checkerboard (so transparency reads as transparency) with an **Upload Image** button that states the required 512×512 transparent PNG and checks both before sending. `npm run nutrition:base-images` is the one-time backfill: it discovers the foods from the database, takes artwork from `--from <dir>` when supplied and otherwise renders a consistent stylized illustration offline (`domain/nutritionImageArt.ts` — no AI integration, per `CLAUDE.md`), uploads and persists each one, skips foods that already have an image, keeps going after a failure and reports discovered/present/generated/uploaded/failed with each failure's id and name. **The run itself is a production step** — it needs the real base library and `CLOUDFLARE_R2_*` credentials, neither of which a PR session has — and is tracked in `docs/go-to-production.md`.)
- **Done (continued)**: #767 (Standing decision on the CodeQL `js/xss-through-dom` alert the browser media-upload helpers raise, written down rather than re-derived per PR: `docs/architecture.md` gains a *Code scanning (CodeQL)* section — the two checks a PR carries (`Analyze` is the scan, `CodeQL` is the results check), the four routes an alert can take in order (fix the code; a human dismissal in Security → Code scanning, which needs `security_events: write` an agent does not have; an owner-authorized merge that records the assessment, which is how #764 landed; narrowing the query suite, for a systematically wrong rule only), and why the detached-media-element flow is a false positive — with the tell that the identical `img.src = url` in `exerciseImageUpload.ts` is unflagged because `new Image()` resolves to a known element type. No suppression comment is ever part of that: `// codeql[rule]` and `// lgtm[rule]` were both tried on the same alert and GitHub honoured neither, so the two inert `// lgtm[js/missing-rate-limiting]` comments on `/sellable-items` and `/taxes` are removed and `api/src/test/codeql-suppressions.unit.test.ts` fails the build if one returns. The rate-limiting claim those comments made is now a go-to-production item: `app.ts` applies a global `apiLimiter` (500 req / 15 min per IP) ahead of every route, so `/sellable-items` and `/taxes` were never the two unthrottled routes the comments implied. No API, schema or UI change.)

- **Done (continued)**: #636 (Payment Providers moved from Finance to Cordel and became real data: migration 175 adds a platform-level `payment_providers` catalogue (no `gym_id` — like `themes`/`charge_types`) with `provider_key` (the adapter `getPaymentProvider()` switches on), a soft-delete, a **single default** enforced by a generated-column unique index, and `gyms.payment_provider_id` **NOT NULL** with a RESTRICT foreign key, backfilled to the seeded `MONEI` row. Superadmin CRUD at `/platform/payment-providers` (inline card UX, no modal) plus `GET .../deployment`, which reports the env-derived credential status the retired Finance page used to render — **credentials stay in environment variables**, the catalogue stores none. Deleting a provider gyms point at is a 409, as is deactivating one; the default cannot be deleted or un-flagged (another provider takes the flag). Cordel → Gyms gains a mandatory **Payment Provider** dropdown, pre-populated with the default. The Finance nav entry, the `/payments/payment-providers` redirect and the `financials.payment_providers` feature flag are gone. Billing itself still resolves the adapter from `PAYMENT_PROVIDER`; pointing it at the gym's row is deferred until a second adapter exists — see `docs/go-to-production.md`.)

- **Done (continued)**: #675 (View Audit Log on every entity's Details view: the deep link #642 added to the Member Details modal is now offered by every Details view in the admin app, through one shared `apps/admin/src/components/ViewAuditLogButton.tsx`. The component owns the permission rule (admin or superadmin, and the `system` / `system.audit` flags, matching the sidebar) and the URL shape (`?entity_type=<canonical>&entity_id=<pk>`), so no Details view hand-rolls either; `scope="platform"` points the three platform-level entities — Gyms, base Themes and the Cordel Nutrition Library — at Cordel → Audit Log instead of the gym one. Filtering is always by id, never by display name. 23 Details views covered: Members, Membership Plans, Promotions, Exercises, Assigned Plans, Tax Rates, Products, Workout Templates, Training Plans and Templates, Activity Types, Professional Services, Centers, Spaces, Staff, Themes, Billing Events, Nutrition Plans and Templates, both Nutrition Libraries, Gyms and platform Themes. On the API side `AUDIT_ENTITY_REGISTRY` gains the nine entity types those views deep-link that `recordAudit()` already wrote but the registry did not know (`activity_type`, `theme`, `tax_rate`, `gym_charge`, `professional_service`, `nutrition_library_item`, `nutrition_plan_template`, `member_nutrition_plan`, `staff`) — without an entry they resolved no `entity_name` and were missing from the `GET /audit-logs/meta` dropdown the deep link preselects. No schema change and no audit-writing behaviour changed. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/view-audit-log-everywhere.test.ts` and `api/src/test/audit-registry.test.ts`)

- **Done (continued)**: #667 (Fix Info Toast Styling: `toast(message, type?)` in `apps/admin/src/components/Toast.tsx` defaults `type` to `'error'`, so a confirmation that omits the second argument renders with the red border and the ✕ icon — which is what "Diagnostics copied to clipboard." did. Frontend only: no API, schema or i18n change, and the component's three variants are untouched. The three confirmations left on the default now pass `'success'` — the storage-diagnostics copy in Cordel → Gyms (`system/gyms/page.tsx`) and the manual-payment and successful-retry toasts in Payments → Billing Events. Genuine failures keep the error variant, including `retry_failed` / `retry_failed_paused` and the Nutrition Plan Templates tree's blocking client-side validation messages. New `apps/admin/src/test/toast-severity.test.ts` parses every `toast(` call in the admin app (balanced-paren argument split, so multi-line calls are read correctly) and fails when a message whose wording says it succeeded is left on the default variant, when a message whose wording says it failed is given `'success'`/`'info'`, or when an unknown `ToastType` literal is passed; `docs/feature-patterns.md` records the convention alongside the existing error-toast rules)

- **Done (continued)**: #670 (Rename “Amount” to “Price” in Products: the field the Products catalogue calls `amount` is the item's price, so every label that named it now reads **Price** / **Precio** / **Preu** — the `PRICE` list column (still between `UNITS` and `TAX RATE`) and the four form/detail sites that render the field: the inline create form, the inline edit form, the expanded row's `BILLING CONFIGURATION` section and the Details modal. i18n-only: `sellable_items.col_amount`/`label_amount` were renamed to `col_price`/`label_price` in en/es/ca — matching the `*_price` key convention the `plans`, `prices`, `class_packages` and `memberships` namespaces already use — and the page reads the new keys. Nothing behind the label moves: the API still returns and accepts `amount`, both forms still submit `amount`, `fmtAmount()` and the tax-inclusive list cell (`amount_incl_tax` + the `taxIncluded`/`taxExcluded` suffix) are untouched, and there is no migration and no change to the DB column, the billing path or the VAT computation. Per the ticket, `Amount` labels that mean a transaction amount rather than a catalogue price are deliberately left alone — Members → Payments, Payments → Billing Events, and the membership ledger — as is the dead `gym_charges` i18n namespace, whose page has been a redirect to Products since #613 and which no component reads. New `apps/admin/src/test/sellable-items-price-label.test.ts` (6 cases: the label in all three locales, the old keys gone from both the locale files and the page, no locale value reintroducing “Amount”/“Importe”/“Import” anywhere in the namespace, the four `label_price` call sites and the column's position, the payload/formatting left intact, and the unrelated `Amount` labels preserved); `sellable-items-column-alignment.test.ts` (#637) follows the key rename)

- **Done (continued)**: #668 (Move Gym Storage Root Under `gyms/`: `buildGymFolderPrefix()` in `api/src/infra/storage.ts` now returns `gyms/<gym_id>-<sanitized_gym_name>` instead of `<gym_id>-<sanitized_gym_name>`, so a gym's root inside the shared R2 bucket is `<bucket>/gyms/<gym_id>-<gym_name>/`. That one helper is the single place the root is built: `POST /platform/gyms/:id/storage/initialize` captures its result in `gyms.storage_folder_prefix`, and both `initializeGymBucket()`'s folder markers and every later `uploadGymImage()` key hang off the stored value — so the `gyms/` root propagates with no second concatenation site and nothing below the gym root changes. Purely an object-key change: bucket (`CLOUDFLARE_R2_BUCKET`), endpoint, credentials, the `{gym_id}-{gym_name}` naming convention, the folder list and the diagnostics/error paths are all untouched, and no migration runs. Per the ticket's explicit constraint, **existing gyms are not migrated** — a gym initialized before this keeps its old captured prefix and its objects (re-running Initialize Cloudflare Bucket deliberately reuses the captured prefix rather than recomputing it), so the bucket can hold both shapes; `docs/go-to-production.md` §1 carries the decision to copy-and-rewrite or accept the split before launch. Unit coverage in `api/src/test/storage.test.ts`: the new prefix shape, every folder marker under `gyms/`, and an upload key under `gyms/`)

- **Done (continued)**: #669 (Remove “Manage” Button from Gym Edit View: the expanded read-only row in Cordel → Gyms (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/system/gyms/page.tsx`) ended with a `Manage` button under `NOTES` that called `setActiveGymId(gym.id)` and pushed the superadmin to `/{locale}/members` — an impersonation shortcut the ticket retires. Frontend-only deletion: the button, its wrapper `div`, the now-orphaned `handleManage()` helper, and `setActiveGymId` from the page's `useGym()` destructure (it had no other caller in the file). `NOTES` now ends after the notes paragraph with no trailing gap. Nothing else moves — the notes field, the gym information rows, the `Initialize Cloudflare Bucket` button, the Details/Edit/Duplicate/Delete context menu, routing, permissions and the API are untouched, and `setActiveGymId` itself stays in the gym context for the gym switcher and its other callers. No migration, no API change, no i18n key — the label was a hardcoded string, not a translation)

- **Done (continued)**: #672 (Align Workout Block Configuration by Type: a Workout Block's block-level numeric configuration now follows its Block Type — Standard, Superset, Triset and Giant Set have none, Circuit has `Rounds`, EMOM and AMRAP have `Minutes`, Tabata has `Intervals` — instead of every type but EMOM/AMRAP offering `Rounds`. `blockFieldConfig.ts` replaces the per-field `BLOCK_TYPE_FIELDS`/`isBlockFieldVisible` list with `BLOCK_TYPE_CONFIG`, one entry per type naming the column it persists to, its label/summary i18n keys and the stored-per-displayed unit, plus `getBlockConfig()`, `blockConfigInput()` (stored → input) and `blockConfigPatch()` (input → block patch). The three editors read it and nothing else: the Workouts builder (`WorkoutBlockBuilder.tsx`, used by Training → Training Plans), the Workout Templates tree (`WorkoutTemplateTree.tsx`) and the member Training Plan blocks modal (`members/PlanWorkoutBlocksModal.tsx`), as does `blockSummary()` in `summaries.ts` (so the Training Plan Template tree's chips follow too). No schema and no API change: `Minutes` is a display unit over the existing `duration_seconds` column (÷60 in, ×60 out) and Tabata's `Intervals` are stored in `rounds`, so blocks written before this read back unchanged. `work_seconds` and `rest_seconds` are no longer offered by any type — the columns and any values already in them stay, unread and uncleared, per the “don't clear hidden fields” rule in `docs/feature-patterns.md`; the block-level `col_duration`/`col_work_seconds`/`col_rest_seconds` and `summary_work`/`summary_rest` labels are dropped from en/es/ca and `col_minutes`/`col_intervals`/`summary_intervals` added. Per the ticket, `Sets` stays an exercise-level field and Target/Unit/Rest on the exercise row are untouched. New `apps/admin/src/test/workout-block-config.test.ts` (20 cases: the per-type map, the minutes and rounds conversions and their round-trip, empty/NaN input → null, hidden columns left to the caller, the three editors routing every write through `blockConfigPatch`, Sets still exercise-level, and the label keys present in all three locales with the retired ones gone))

- **Done (continued)**: #674 (Payments → Dashboard: a read-only metric page under Payments with four Billing Event counters — Scheduled This Month, and Total / Failed / Successful Last Month. New router `api/src/api/payments-dashboard.ts` exposing a single `GET /payments/dashboard/summary`, mounted in `app.ts` **before** `/payments` so it is gated by its own `payments.dashboard` flag (migration 171 seeds the key enabled) rather than by `payments.transactions` — the Dashboard survives Transactions or Billing Events being switched off, and can be switched off on its own. Last month's three cards come from one grouped query over `billing_events` (`created_at` inside the previous calendar month, one `WHERE be.gym_id = ?`) whose groups are mapped through the shared `deriveBillingEventStatus()` (#640), so the status vocabulary is never re-spelled as SQL and the cards agree with the Billing Events page; `total_last_month` counts every ledger row, so it is not necessarily failed + successful (an `adjustment` or `status_changed` row is neither). Scheduled This Month reuses the page's projection from `user_memberships.next_billing_date` + `billing_policies.recurring_billing_interval`, counting the projected dates that fall inside the current month and skipping dates the nightly run has not caught up with, exactly as the `scheduled` rows on that page do. Month boundaries are derived in UTC in one exported `monthWindows()` so the SQL ranges and the JS projection share a clock, and the API returns the window it counted over so the page's period labels can't drift into the browser's time zone. Frontend `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/payments/dashboard/page.tsx` follows the #638 Finance Dashboard card wall; the nav entry carries `featureKey: 'payments.dashboard'` and the page itself bounces a direct URL when the flag is off, applying the same superadmin-bypass-unless-impersonating rule as the sidebar. New `payments_dashboard` namespace in en/es/ca; no Billing Event or payment-processing behaviour changed. Covered by `api/src/test/payments-dashboard.test.ts`)

- **Done (continued)**: #673 (Activate/Deactivate Toggle in the Exercise Context Menu: a gym exercise's ⋮ menu now carries a status toggle between `Duplicate` and `Delete` — `Deactivate` when the row is active, `Activate` when it is inactive — so a status change no longer requires opening the inline editor. Frontend-only: `handleToggleStatus()` in `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/exercises/page.tsx` reuses `PUT /exercises/:id` with a status-only body, which the router already applies on its own (`status = COALESCE(?, status)`, every other column guarded by `'field' in req.body`, muscles and result types left untouched when absent), then reloads the list so the `StatusBadge` and the status filter follow. No confirmation dialog, per the ticket — the delete confirmation is the only one on the page. The toggle carries the same `canWrite` gate and read-only tooltip as the other write actions, and an open inline editor has its form status synced so saving it cannot revert the toggle. Base exercises (`gym_id IS NULL`) keep their `Details` + `Clone to gym` menu — the router answers 403 for a gym-side write to them — and the Cordel → Base Exercises page is unchanged. New `exercises.activate`/`deactivate` keys in en/es/ca; no migration, no API change. Covered by `apps/admin/src/test/exercise-status-toggle.test.ts` (9 cases: the labels in all three locales, the status-conditional menu entry, the write gate, the base-exercise menu, the status-only payload, the editor sync and the absence of a second confirmation) plus a status-only `PUT` case in `api/src/test/exercises.test.ts`)

- **Done (continued)**: #643 (Translate Nutrition Library Content: migration 166 adds `nutrition_library_item_translations` (`item_id FK→nutrition_library_items` CASCADE, `locale`, `name`, PK `(item_id, locale)`, `KEY nlit_locale_name`) — the same catalogue+junction shape as categories (#501) and qualities (#293) — and seeds Spanish + Catalan for all 32 system items (`gym_id IS NULL`) via `INSERT IGNORE ... SELECT` matched on the base name, so a re-run, a renamed item or an already-edited translation is a no-op. `nutrition_library_items.name` stays the base (English) value, the uniqueness key and the fallback for any locale with no row. New `api/src/infra/locale.ts` resolves the request locale from `x-locale`, then `Accept-Language` (q-value ordered, `es-ES`→`es`), then `DEFAULT_LOCALE`, always narrowing to the `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` allowlist (env-configured, default `en,es,ca`); `domain/nutritionLibrary.ts` gains `localizedNameSql`/`localizedNameExpr` (a `COALESCE` subquery, a bare column for the base locale), `loadTranslationsMap`, `replaceTranslations` and `validateTranslations`. Every read surface resolves names per request — `nutrition-library.ts`, `platform-nutrition-library.ts`, `nutrition-plan-templates.ts`, `platform-nutrition-plan-templates.ts`, `member-nutrition-plans.ts` and `me.ts` (`GET /me/nutrition-plan`) — with library list rows now carrying `display_name` (localized) and `translations` next to the base `name`, and list search/ordering following the displayed locale. Superadmins author translations on Cordel → Base Nutrition Library (one field per translatable locale, blank = fall back) through `POST`/`PUT` `/platform/nutrition-library` plus a new `PUT /platform/nutrition-library/:id/translations` sub-resource and `GET /platform/nutrition-library/locales`. Gym-created items keep a single entered name shown in every locale. Category and quality names stay slug-keyed and translated in the apps' locale files, where en/es/ca were already complete. Both apps send `x-locale` from `useLocale()` (added to both proxies' header allowlists). 23 unit cases in `locale.test.ts`; integration coverage added to `platform-nutrition-library.test.ts`, `nutrition-library.test.ts` and `me-nutrition-plan.test.ts`)

- **Done (continued)**: #644 (Review and Classify Nutrition Library Nutritional Qualities: migration 167 completes the partial backfill migration 127 (#350) started. `fat` and `fiber` existed in the `nutritional_qualities` catalogue since #501 but had **zero** item assignments, and only 20 `protein`/`carbohydrate` links existed at all. `nutrition_library_items` stores no macro values — the row is `id, gym_id, name, status, image_url, created_at, modified_at` — so, per the ticket thread's answers, the classification is made by food from general nutritional composition and seeded in a migration, where the per-item decisions are reviewable in the diff and versioned with the schema (no numeric columns added; that stays a separate ticket if ever wanted). Stated criterion, indicative per 100 g of the food as normally served: protein ≥ 10 g, carbohydrate ≥ 10 g, fat ≥ 8 g, fiber ≥ 2 g, with wholegrain/whole-plant foods marked for fiber and refined-grain staples not, so the borderline cases are decided by a rule rather than by rounding. All 32 system items are in the table, including the seven that qualify for nothing (Water/Coffee/Tea/Mustard/Hot Sauce/Tomato Sauce/Yogurt Sauce) so the review is complete on its face; each row carries a one-line note. 19 new links: `fat` on Beef/Salmon/Eggs/Tofu/Olive Oil/Nuts/Dairy, `fiber` on Brown Rice/Vegetables/Salad/Sweet Potatoes/Quinoa/Fruit/Oats/Nuts, plus `protein` on Dairy and `carbohydrate` on Juice. Matching is by item name and covers **gym-created** items as well as system ones, but is **additive only** (`INSERT IGNORE`) — re-reviewing 127's 20 links found none that contradict the criterion, and a name-matched heuristic must not delete a tag a gym set by hand; non-active items are skipped, and it is a one-time review rather than a standing rule (an item created later is untouched). Note the `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` collation makes the name match case- and accent-insensitive, so a gym's "Salmón" is classified as "Salmon". `down()` is deliberately narrower than `up()` — system items only, and never 127's pairs — because `INSERT IGNORE` keeps no record of which links it actually created, so a name-and-slug delete cannot distinguish its own row from one an admin set by hand; leaving an extra tag on a gym item is recoverable from the UI, deleting a curated one is not. No schema, API, UI or i18n change — the `quality_*` labels and the read/write surface (`domain/nutritionLibrary.ts`, both Nutrition Library pages) already handled all four slugs. New `nutrition-library-quality-classification.test.ts` (6 DB-free unit cases: full system-item coverage, valid slugs only, a note per item, every quality used, no 127 assignment lost, the untagged set), plus 5 integration cases appended to `nutrition-library.test.ts` asserting the seeded result in the migrated database and through `GET /nutrition-library`)

- **Done (continued)**: #640 (Billing Events Context Menu, Details, Retry Payment and Manual payment: migration 165 adds `modified_at`/`modified_by_user_id` to both `billing_events` and `payment_requests` (Clerk-id actor column, matching `billing_events.actor_user_id`, resolved to a name through `gym_memberships.name`), `failure_code`/`failure_message`/`notes`/`attempt` to `payment_requests`, and widens `chk_payment_requests_source` to `admin|customer|billing_run|retry|manual`. A Billing Event's **status is now derived from its latest linked Payment Transaction** (`domain/billingEventStatus.ts` — pure, unit-tested: `completed`→paid, `failed`/`expired`→failed, `pending`→pending), falling back to the `event_type` mapping when the event has no transaction; `pending` joins the status vocabulary of the list filter. New routes on `paymentsRouter`: `GET /payments/billing-events/:id` (read-only Details — id, member, plan, amount, currency, status, charge type, source, Created/Modified At+By, Next Payment, failure reason, receipt, `can_retry`/`can_record_manual_payment`), `POST /payments/billing-events/:id/retry` and `POST /payments/billing-events/:id/manual-payment` (both `requireModuleWrite('PAYMENTS')`). Neither action ever appends a Billing Event: they add Payment Transactions against the existing `billing_event_id`, so the append-only ledger row is never rewritten. Retry (issue Q3 option B) fires the MIT charge and, on rejection, once more — two rejections pause the assigned plan through `recordStatusChange`, and every covered member's derived payment status follows because `GET /members` now resolves `payment_status` through `user_membership_members` as well as `pr.member_id` (two-person/family plans bill once, against the owner). A successful retry or manual payment advances `next_billing_date`/`last_billed_at` exactly like the nightly run, guarded by a 409 on an event that already has a completed transaction so one charge can never advance the schedule twice. `audit_logs` records the two manual interventions (`retry_payment`, `manual_payment`, added to `AUDIT_ACTIONS`) with previous/new status and the attempt results. The nightly run now also stores the provider's rejection reason on the failed `payment_requests` row, not only in the ledger row's notes. Admin UI: a `ContextMenu` per real row (Details always; Retry Payment + Manual payment only when the row is actionable), Details expanding the same row read-only and the Manual payment card rendered inline inside it — no modal; read-only PAYMENTS roles see the menu with the write items disabled)

- **Done (continued)**: #634 (Member Membership Configuration and Consolidated Billing Simulation — stage 3 of the #629 → #631 → #634 chain, whose engine and Additional Periodic Services shipped in the first two. Migration 172 narrows migration 007's one-active-Membership-per-Member unique index from `(active_member_key)` to `(active_member_key, membership_plan_id)`, so a Member may hold **several active Membership Plans in parallel, one per Plan** — the thread's answer to the blocking question. The index swap needs no data migration (the old guarantee implies the new one) and touches no row; the rollback refuses with an actionable message if any Member already holds two. Adding a Plan is purely additive (§14): `POST /user-memberships` no longer supersedes anything, and "Assign New Plan" stays as the separate, explicit supersede action. `POST /user-memberships` and `/:id/assign-new-plan` now reject a Plan that is not Active + Public (§2, `planAssignabilityError()`); `GET /membership-plans` gains an `enrollment_status` filter, and the Members page's plan fetch — previously `?status=active`, a parameter the endpoint does not read, so every plan came back — now asks for `lifecycle_status=active&enrollment_status=public`. New read-only `GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/configuration` (`member-membership-configuration.ts`) returns `{plans, promotions, services}` in one call — every assignment newest-first with `is_live` and its `activity_allowances`, plus the live assignments' Promotions and Additional Services, each carrying `user_membership_id` and `plan_name`. It contains no business logic: every write stays on the existing per-Assigned-Plan routes. `MemberExpandedRow.tsx` is restructured into the ticket's four independent sections — `MEMBERSHIP PLANS` (`MemberMembershipPlans.tsx`, inline "+ Add Membership Plan" with radio-button Plan selection), `PROMOTIONS` (`MemberPromotions.tsx`, Member-level, each row naming its Plan, stacking rules live in the picker), `ADDITIONAL SERVICES` (`MemberAdditionalServices.tsx`, reusing #631's inline editor once per live Plan) and `BILLING SIMULATION` — none nested inside a plan card (§13) and no modal anywhere (§15). A change in any of the first three re-reads the configuration and remounts the simulation (§12), whose lines now name the Plan that generated each charge (§7). The simulation itself already consolidated every live assignment from #629/#631, so §5–§11 needed no engine change. Knock-on: `POST /me/payment-requests` stopped picking "the" active membership with `LIMIT 1` — it takes an optional `user_membership_id` and answers `409 multiple_active_memberships` otherwise. New `members.*` keys in en/es/ca; new `api/src/test/member-membership-configuration.test.ts`, plus cases in `user-memberships.test.ts`, `membership-plans.test.ts` and `membership-promotions` coverage for parallel plans, the Active+Public gate and the new-members rule. §3's "Promotion `Only applicable for new members` is respected" landed as a follow-up once the thread defined "new": *a member that books his/her first membership plan in 12 months* — a Member away for longer than that counts as new again. `domain/newMemberEligibility.ts` holds the rule (an Assigned Plan disqualifies its Member when it is still live, started inside the window, or ended/was closed inside it; a row superseded by Assign New Plan carries no end date and is dated by the assignment that replaced it) and `api/new-member-eligibility.ts` reads the Member's assignments for it. Enforced at every apply path — `POST /user-memberships/:id/promotions`, `applyPromotionToMembership()` (assign-new-plan, `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`'s auto-apply, `POST /payments/apply-promotion`) and #628's up-front `validatePromotionSelection()`, which now takes the `member_id` — always excluding the assignment the Promotion is being applied to, so a Member's first Plan never disqualifies itself. The configuration read reports it per plan as `new_member_eligible`, which is what the PROMOTIONS picker disables the option on. No migration: the column already exists. Note the flag is `DEFAULT 1`, so every Promotion created before this carries it — test fixtures that are not about the rule now set it to 0 explicitly. Also unchanged, and out of scope: the Assigned Plan snapshot architecture (#635).)
- **Done (continued)**: #631 (Additional Periodic Services on an Assigned Plan: migration 164 adds `user_membership_services` (gym_id, `user_membership_id FK→user_memberships` CASCADE, `gym_charge_id FK→gym_charges` with no cascade, quantity, `starts_at`, `ends_at`, CHECKs `quantity > 0` and `ends_at >= starts_at`, plus a VIRTUAL generated `open_service_key` under a unique index so two concurrent POSTs can't attach the same item twice), attaching recurring Products directly to the Assigned Plan — independent of the Membership Plan's included benefits and of Promotion benefits, which keep their own tables. The Product stays the source of truth for name/price/`billing_frequency` (read live, never snapshotted); only items `classifySellableItem()` classifies as `periodical` are attachable, and only while the plan still bills. New `api/src/api/user-membership-services.ts` router mounted at `/user-memberships/:id/services` (GET/POST/DELETE, PAYMENTS gate + `payments.transactions` flag); the services are also embedded in `GET /user-memberships/:id` as `additional_services`. Removal is future-only: DELETE stamps `ends_at = today` so charges already produced stand, and only deletes outright when billing hadn't started. The Billing Simulation (#629) now projects each service as its own stream at the item's own frequency and price × quantity, over its effective window, with no Promotion benefit ever applied to it — a service added after the plan started bills from its effective date, and a removed one disappears after its end date. New `ADDITIONAL PERIODIC SERVICES` inline section (no modal) in `AdditionalPeriodicServices.tsx`, rendered both in the Assigned Plan expanded row and in the Member page's current plan card, where an add/remove also remounts the Billing Simulation so it recalculates immediately; new `assigned_plans_page.services_*` / `section_additional_services` and `members.section_additional_services` i18n keys in en/es/ca; 9 new unit cases in `billing-simulation.test.ts`, a new integration file `user-membership-services.test.ts`, 2 new simulation cases in `user-memberships.test.ts`, and 9 structural cases in the admin app's `additional-periodic-services.test.ts`)

- **Done (continued)**: #645 (Registration endpoint keyed by gym id + health check: `POST /public/gyms/:slug/registrations` becomes `POST /public/gyms/:gymRef/registrations`, where `:gymRef` is `{gymId}-{gym-name}` — `parseGymRef` resolves the leading `CHAR(36)` UUID and ignores the readable half, so two gyms with the same name can no longer share an endpoint and a rename never breaks a configured website. A reference that doesn't start with a gym id still resolves as `gyms.slug`, so WordPress installs configured before this change keep registering; `GET /system/website-integration` now advertises the new form (`gym_ref` + `endpoint_path` / `endpoint_url`, name half URL-encoded). Second half: exactly `{ name: 'test', email: '' }` is a health check — after the key check and gym resolution it returns `200 { ok: true, health_check: true }` before validation, creating no member, no invitation and no email, and skipping the per-gym daily quota (per-IP limiter still applies); `isHealthCheckBody` can't collide with a real registration because the schema requires a non-empty email.
