Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/sellable-items-mandatory-badge.test.ts` (10).)
- **Done (continued)**: #897 (**Promotion component Edit actions move inside Edit mode.** #627 gave each part of an expanded Promotion its own section-level `Edit` button, and they were reachable straight from the read-only card: a staff member reading a Promotion could open the Session, One-off, Periodical or Membership Fee editor without ever passing through `⋮ → Edit`. The card now has one Edit mode (`editingId`), entered only from the context menu, and every editor in the expanded body is gated on it — the main configuration renders as a form while it lasts, and each Benefit section shows its own `Edit` button, unchanged in look, label and placement (§3/§6: the button is not redesigned, only its availability changes). `editingSection` became `openSection` and lost its `'main'` member, since the main configuration is no longer one of the mutually exclusive sections; the per-section saves, their endpoints, `enterSectionEdit`'s re-read and #625's duration clamp are untouched. Cancelling or saving a Benefit section closes that section alone and stays in Edit mode (its error line is its own, so a failed section save no longer prints under the main form); cancelling or saving Edit mode itself returns the Promotion to its **read-only expanded** view rather than collapsing the row — #816's rule, now applied to Promotions — with every section editor closed. Creation is unchanged: the new-Promotion row is still one form with one Save, its section headers carry no Edit button, and cancelling it still discards the row. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/promotions-expanded-read-only.test.ts` (8), with #627's `promotions-section-editing.test.ts` updated to the new state names.)
- **Done (continued)**: #900 (**Promotions expire automatically after their End Date.** A new `promotions.lifecycle_status` value, `expired`, and a scheduled sweep that writes it. The two states stay distinct on purpose (§1, §4): `inactive` is an administrative decision, `expired` is the calendar catching up with a Promotion left switched on — before this ticket a Promotion whose window closed in July still read *Active* in the list while every apply path already refused it (`validatePromotionSelection` / `applyPromotionToMembership`: *outside its active window*), so the status column and the behaviour disagreed. `expired` is exactly "no longer applicable", which is also why §7 needed no new code: both paths require `lifecycle_status = 'active'`. The rule lives once in `api/src/domain/promotionLifecycle.ts` — `promotionExpiryWhereSql(alias)` (only `active` expires, `ends_at IS NOT NULL` for §6, `ends_at < UTC_TIMESTAMP()` so the comparison stays in the database's clock, the same end-date semantics the apply paths already use) plus `PROMOTION_LIFECYCLE_STATUSES`, which `GET /promotions?lifecycle_status=` and the write validation now both read. Excluding `expired` from the clause is what makes the sweep idempotent (§2): its second run matches nothing. The trigger is `POST /promotion-lifecycle/run` (`api/src/api/promotion-lifecycle.ts`), mounted beside the other internal runners — system-wide, so it cannot sit on the tenant-scoped `/promotions` router — and added as the **third step of `billing-run.yml`**, whose `BILLING_INTERNAL_SECRET` it shares rather than introducing a third secret to provision and rotate (#783 documents the two that exist); a job of its own would get a secret of its own. No run-log claim and no audit row, both deliberate: `claimRun()`/`finishRun()` (#780) exist to stop two *charging* runs overlapping, and `recordAudit()` needs a `tenantCtx` actor a scheduled job does not have (the nightly billing run records `billing_events` for the same reason). Only `lifecycle_status` is written (§8) — the dates, the Billing & Duration months, the benefit sections, the targeted Plans and every `user_membership_promotions` application are untouched, and an application prices from its own snapshot (#635 §16), so a Promotion expiring re-prices nobody. Migration 202 only widens `chk_promotions_lifecycle_status`; **nothing is backfilled** (a statement that expired every past-dated Promotion at deploy time is a data decision taken invisibly — the sweep does it on its first run, counted in the workflow log), and its `down` clears the `expired` rows back to **`active`** before narrowing the CHECK — MySQL validates existing rows and DDL is not transactional, so they have to go, and `active` is both where they were before #900 and the only value that survives the round trip, since the sweep's expirable set is `active` alone and `inactive` would strand every one of them in the status that means a human switched it off. Admin: the page now has a **filter** list (Active / Inactive / Expired, §10 — never lumped together) and an **edit** list (Active / Inactive), because `Expired` is something the sweep writes and not a second way of saying Inactive; an already-expired row renders its status as a *disabled* option so saving its other fields cannot quietly reactivate it, and choosing Active is how a gym revives one (the sweep expires it again unless the End Date moved). `StatusBadge` already had an `expired` colour and `status.expired` already existed in en/es/ca, so §9 needed no new key. `POST /:id/duplicate` still copies the source's status and dates verbatim — Duplicate is a copy. Tests: `promotion-lifecycle.unit.test.ts` (9 pure cases, including that migration 202 permits every status the union declares), `promotion-expiry.test.ts` (15 integration cases — the six §13 scenarios, auth, cross-gym, §8's untouched columns and the API surface) and `apps/admin/src/test/promotions-expired-status.test.ts` (10).)
- **Done (continued)**: #896 (**A Product configured in a Promotion or a Membership Plan carries a pricing treatment, not just a quantity.** Stage 1 of the plan agreed on the issue thread: schema + vocabulary, nothing reads it yet. Migration 203 puts an `(action, value)` pair on twelve relationship tables — `promotion_{session,oneoff,periodical}`, `membership_plan_{session,oneoff,periodical}` and the six assignment-side snapshots that have to carry it for the Assigned Plan rule — and on nothing else (§12: the global Product is untouched). Two option sets, declared once in `api/src/domain/sellableItemBenefitActions.ts` and mirrored by the `chk_<table>_action` CHECK beside each table: five actions for a Promotion, three for a Plan, since §16 says Plans cannot configure *Fixed discount* or *Fixed Price*. The vocabulary is `PromotionBenefitAction`, not a new enum, and `applyLineBenefit()` delegates to `applyPeriodBenefit()` — §11's "do not introduce a second independent pricing system". New rows default to `no_benefit` (§13); rows that already exist on the **Promotion** side are backfilled to `waive`, because a Promotion grant is an implicit waive today and leaving them neutral would, at stage 3, start charging members for items their Promotion gives them free (the thread's Q1 answer). Stage 2 = API validation, persistence and the snapshot writers; stage 3 = the pricing cutover in `domain/billingSimulation.ts`; stage 4 = the dropdown and its conditional value input in the shared `SellableItemBenefits.tsx`, plus the labels-only terminology sweep of §3. **Stage 2** made the pair writable and readable: the six replace-all `PUT`s (`/promotions/:id/{session,oneoff,periodical}-benefits` and the Membership Plan equivalents) validate the line's `(action, value)` against its own context through `parseSellableItemBenefitInput()` and persist it, every read reports it normalized (`value` a number, not mysql2's `DECIMAL` string, and an action the context may not configure read back as the neutral default), and both Duplicate paths copy it verbatim. The load-bearing rule is that a `PUT` which does **not** name the pair keeps what the line is stored with rather than resetting it — a replace-all from a client that only knows `gym_charge_id` + `quantity`, which is every client until stage 4, would otherwise wipe migration 203's `waive` backfill and start charging at stage 3 for what a Promotion gives away. Clearing a treatment stays possible and stays explicit (`action: 'no_benefit'`). #893's mandatory rule carries through unchanged: an implicit row reads `no_benefit`, and a mandatory item the client dropped is re-added naming no treatment, so preserving it can never reprice it. **Stage 3** is the pricing cutover: `domain/billingSimulation.ts` stops hard-coding "a Plan benefit is charged and a Promotion grant is free" and reads each row's own pair instead. A Product line is now priced in two steps — the item's own treatment applies to the whole line (`applyLineBenefit()`: the **line**, `unit × quantity`, per the thread's Q2 answer, never the unit) for the life of the assignment, and each covering Promotion grant's treatment is folded on top through `applyPeriodBenefit()` for the periods or units that grant covers, so two Promotions covering the same period both count and a `waive` over anything still gives 0. Nothing an existing member holds moves by a cent: migration 203 backfilled every promotion-side row to `waive` (which is what the hard-coded free coverage meant) and every plan-side row to `no_benefit` (the normal price), and the whole pre-ticket test suite passes unchanged apart from the label a line reports — a grant now says `waive` rather than the pre-#896 `included`, which meant the same thing when a grant could carry no pair. Two rules are load-bearing: a Plan's own treatment never makes a charge **promotional**, because it never ends and the projection's horizon (#629 §6) would otherwise run every discounted item to the 36-month safety cap — it is the same reasoning that keeps the Personal Membership Fee Benefit (#772) out of that flag; and a grant configured `no_benefit` covers the period without changing its price or its horizon, since it discounts nothing. On a one-off or session line the units are allocated grant by grant in order, capped at the quantity actually billed, so two Promotions granting 4 sessions each on a 6-session Plan treat 4 and 2 rather than 8 of 6; whatever is left over is priced by the Plan's own treatment. The three loaders that feed the engine (`loadPlanBenefitsForSimulation`, `loadPromotionGrantSnapshots` and the live-Promotion fallback in `api/billing-simulation.ts`) project the pair through `toSellableItemBenefit()`, each in its own context, so a `fixed_price` that reached a Plan row cannot price there. The pair is a **required** field of `SimulationGrant`/`SimulationPlanBenefit` for the reason `personalFeeBenefit` is: a loader that forgot it would silently charge for what a Promotion gives away. No migration, no API change, no frontend change — the dropdown that configures any of this is stage 4, so until it lands a **newly** created grant defaults to `no_benefit` (§13) and is charged, which is the ticket's intended default and harmless while there is no production environment. Tests: nine new cases in `billing-simulation.test.ts` (57 total) covering waive/percentage/fixed-price/fixed-discount on both shapes, stacking, the horizon rules and the per-grant unit allocation. **Stage 4** is the UI, and it is where the two terminologies finally differ on screen: the shared `SellableItemBenefits.tsx` editor gains an action dropdown and, beside it, a value input that appears only for the three actions that take one (§6) and names itself after the action — *Promotion (%)*, *Promotion amount*, *Promotion price* — because one column header could not say all three. The option list is per context: `benefitContext="promotion"` offers all five, `benefitContext="plan"` the three §16 allows, and omitting the prop renders no column at all, which is what leaves the Assigned Plan snapshot editor exactly as it was (its `PUT` takes quantity alone, so a dropdown there would change nothing). The lists are mirrored for the browser in `apps/admin/src/lib/sellableItemBenefitActions.ts` — a **third** place a new action goes, beside the API declaration and the CHECK, which `sellable-item-benefit-actions-ui.test.ts` asserts by reading the API's source. Two rules from §16 are implemented where they cannot drift: a typed value is **kept** in the draft while the action changes, so switching away and back restores it, and `toBenefitItems()` submits only the value the selected action takes; and a line whose draft carries no action at all still sends a quantity-only payload, because stage 2's `PUT`s read that as *keep what is stored*. The Promotions page stopped carrying its own copy of the grid with this stage — `renderSellableItemBenefitEditor` / `…View` are now thin wrappers over the shared component, which is what makes the new field exist once rather than twice. §3's sweep is labels-only, as agreed on the thread: the three remaining `promotions` strings that said "Benefit" now read *Item* and *No promotion* in en/es/ca, and a test fails if any `promotions` label says it again — route paths, table names and locale *key* names are untouched, so the same stored `no_benefit` reads as *No promotion* on one screen and *No benefit* on the other.)

- **Done (continued)**: #901 (**One themed subsection `Edit` button for Membership Plans and Promotions.** The same action — the `Edit` beside a subsection header inside a card's Edit mode — was styled twice and differently: Promotions rendered a filled `btnSmall('#6c63ff')`, Membership Plans a bare `linkBtn` text link, on two cards that are otherwise deliberately identical (#879's shared Billing & Duration summary, #816 §14's shared section set). Both now render one component, `apps/admin/src/components/SectionEditButton.tsx`, which owns the geometry (the Promotions filled shape, per §1) and the colours and nothing else. The colours are the Theme's **existing** `primaryButton`/`primaryButtonText` — §4 asks that an existing setting be reused rather than a new one added, and those two were already in the editor's Buttons group and already written by `applyTokens()` as `--gd-primary-btn`/`--gd-primary-btn-text` while being consumed by nothing, the same inert-setting gap #677 closed for `cardBorderRadius`. `primaryButton`'s default *is* `#6c63ff`, so nothing looks different until a gym themes it, and the two literals in the module are CSS `var()` fallbacks for the frames before `applyTokens()` has run, never a second source of truth (§3's "no hardcoded lilac"). Deliberately **not** `--brand`, which is `sidebarSelectedItemBackground` — the sidebar's selected-item colour, not an action colour — and which is what the Plans link read. The shared button resolves no locale key and makes no permission decision (#806's split, and #879's reason: the two pages namespace their keys differently and a Plan says *Edit pricing* where a Promotion says *Edit*), so `label`, `disabled` and `title` arrive decided; §6's availability is untouched, which is the load-bearing half — the button is still **absent** rather than disabled on a read-only expanded card (#897 for Promotions' `onEdit: null`, #816 for the Plan sections' `isEditing &&` gate) and still disabled with `readOnlyTitle` for a role that may not write, with Promotions keeping `edit_busy_hint` while another section is open. Four Plan sections change (Pricing, Billing & Duration, the three Product benefit sections through one JSX site, Centers) and the orphaned `linkBtn` constant is deleted with them. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key, no new Theme setting, and no editing behaviour moved. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/section-edit-button.test.ts` (11), with #897's `promotions-expanded-read-only.test.ts` updated — it pinned the lilac literal it was this ticket's job to remove.)

- **Done (continued)**: #912 (**The Theme editor's own primary actions read the Theme's Primary Button colours.** #901 made the subsection `Edit` button the first consumer of the Buttons group's `primaryButton`/`primaryButtonText`, but the two Theme screens still painted their own primary actions in the legacy hardcoded lilac — so a gym could set *Primary Button = red* in the very editor whose `+ Assign Centers…` and `Save changes` buttons stayed `#6c63ff`. Four buttons move: `+ Assign Centers…` and `Save changes` on Custom Themes (`[locale]/themes`), `Save changes` and `+ Add` on Base Themes (`[locale]/system/themes`). The pair of `var()` fallbacks is now declared **once**, as `primaryActionColors` in `apps/admin/src/components/ui.tsx`, with `primaryBtnStyle()`/`primaryBtnSmall()` spreading it over `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()`'s geometry; `SectionEditButton` derives from `primaryBtnSmall()` and carries no colour literal of its own, since a second spelling of the same fallbacks is the drift #901 existed to remove. Spreading the pair explicitly is **not** the same as dropping `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()`'s argument: their own default is `--brand`, which `applyTokens` maps to `sidebarSelectedItemBackground` — a different Theme setting, and not an action colour. No new Theme setting (§5), no token semantics changed (Out of scope), and each button keeps the `opacity`/`cursor` pair it already rode on, so a Save with nothing to save and a picker on a non-active Theme look exactly as before (§6). Deliberately left: the Cancel (`#888`), upload (`#444`) and remove (`#c0392b`) buttons, which are secondary or destructive (§4), and the *show all centers* disclosure **text link**, which is not a primary action — its natural home is the Links group's `--gd-link`, a different Theme setting and a different ticket. `CrudModal`'s own Save is likewise untouched: it is shared app-wide chrome, not a Theme-editor button. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` (10), with #901's `section-edit-button.test.ts` updated — it pinned the literals this ticket moved into `ui.tsx`.)

- **Done (continued)**: #913 (**The `Mandatory` pill stands out from the `System` one in Products.** #894 put both name-cell badges on the shared neutral `listNameBadgeStyle`, which is the right voice for `System` ("what kind of row is this") but left `Mandatory` reading as part of the row's chrome — grey on light grey beside a grey pill. `listChrome.ts` now also exports `listNameBadgeAccentStyle`, the accent variant a row's attention-worthy metadata wears, and the Products header renders the `Mandatory` badge through it. It is declared as a spread of the neutral style overriding `color` and `background` and nothing else, so the ticket's "existing chip sizing, typography, padding and border radius are preserved" is structural rather than a matching pair of literals: a value changed on the neutral pill still reaches this one. The pair is amber on light amber (`#92400e` on `#fde68a`, ~8:1), distinct from the row surface, the card and the grey `System` pill. No new Theme setting or CSS variable — the neutral pill's own colours are literals too (#894), so the existing token architecture does not require one — and `System`, the two `StatusBadge` columns, the badge's placement, label, meaning and read-only-ness, the two forms' checkboxes and every payload are untouched; Taxes and Professional Services keep the neutral pill. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: six new cases in `apps/admin/src/test/sellable-items-mandatory-badge.test.ts` (16 total), pinning that the accent style is derived rather than restated, that it overrides exactly the two colour properties, that it shares no colour with the `System` pill, and that the other two badge pages stay neutral.)

- **Done (continued)**: #899 (**The Membership Fee Benefit's Duration is picked from a list of the durations the Promotion can carry.** #625 already bounded the benefit at the Promotion's own timeline (Free Period + Paid Duration + Bonus Duration — Pay Beforehand reclassifies paid periods, it never lengthens the Promotion) and enforced it server-side with a 400 on `PUT /promotions/:id/membership-fee-benefit`, so every backend acceptance line of this ticket was already true and no API, migration or validation changed. What was open is §"Existing Membership Fee Promotion duration"'s "no silent truncation": the editor's Duration was a free-text number input that clamped what you typed with `Math.min(raw, max)`, so entering 12 on a 5-period Promotion silently became 5. The owner's answer on the thread was to remove the problem rather than add a validation message — *the selector only displays the valid options* — so the control is now a `<select>` over `1..max` plus the existing `—` (the whole Promotion, which is what a null duration means and what the API stores), and a value the Promotion cannot carry is simply never offered. A Promotion with no periods at all offers `—` alone, which is how a zero-period Promotion keeps a positive Duration off it. The list and the ceiling moved into one pure module beside the page (`apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/promotions/membershipFeeDuration.ts`), which both `promotionDurationFromForm()` and `promotionDurationFromPromo()` now compute through, so the maximum is derived once and still recomputed live from the unsaved edit form. The one clamp that stays is `clampSavedMembershipFeeDuration()`: it is not truncation of user input but the case where the value was valid when it was picked and the Promotion was shortened underneath it, which would otherwise leave a benefit the backend refuses to save. Durations stay **calendar months** (the thread's Q2 answer: "in promotions we assume 1 month") — #892 moved *Membership Plan* durations onto the Plan's Billing frequency and left `promotionTimeline.ts` alone on purpose, so the column keeps its `Duration (months)` label and no locale key changed. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new locale key. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/promotion-membership-fee-duration.test.ts` (13).)
- **Done (continued)**: #915 (**Billing Event Simulation on Membership Plans.** A third read-only projection on the expanded Plan card, answering the question the other two do not: not "what does each billing *period* do to the Membership Fee" (#818's Example Timeline) but **"what is billed, in full, on each *date*"** — one group per billing date, listing the Membership Fee and every Product the Plan carries, each at the price the Plan's own `(action, value)` treatment gives it (#896), mandatory items included (#893), with a per-date total and tax-inclusive amounts. The ticket's load-bearing constraint was "do not implement a second billing engine", so the new pure module `api/src/domain/planBillingEventSimulation.ts` is a thin adapter over the existing `domain/billingSimulation.ts`: it expresses the Plan as the hypothetical assignment a member enrolling today would hold (no Promotions, no Additional Periodic Services, no Personal Membership Fee Benefit — none of those is a property of the Plan), hands it to `computeBillingSimulation()` and does exactly two things of its own — supply the horizon floor and re-group the engine's cadence sections by date. Every date, every amount and every Free / Pre-paid / Pay / Bonus decision stays the engine's and, through it, `classifyPlanDurationPeriod()`'s — the same classifier the nightly run prices a cycle with, so the section cannot advertise a charge the run does not make. The horizon is the ticket's own rule rather than a month count: a new `minimumCycles` option on the engine raises the projection to at least `start + N x cadence` for **every** recurring stream (`SIMULATED_CYCLES = 2`), so a Plan carrying a yearly item runs two years and drags the 4-weekly ones with it, and a lone 4-weekly EUR 70 fee reads Sep 30 / Oct 28 / Nov 25 — the ticket's own example. It only ever *raises* the horizon, so #629 section 6's first-regular-charge rule still carries a six-period Free Period through to its first paid period. Frequencies stay independent (each item is its own stream at its own frequency — nothing is converted to a common cadence), a `Once` item is billed once, and a `Waive`d line is still shown, at EUR 0, beside the regular price it would otherwise have cost. Tax is handled by running the whole projection in VAT-inclusive euros — the router grosses the Plan's price and each item's price up first, which is exact because a Plan may only configure `no_benefit` / `waive` / `percentage_discount` (#896 section 16) and its durations only waive outright — so no tax arithmetic happens in the projection or in the page (#817), and an item with no tax rate contributes its stored amount exactly as `formatPlanCurrentPrice()` falls back. `enrichPlan` embeds the result as `billing_event_simulation` and `GET /membership-plans/:id/billing-event-simulation` serves the same calculation, mirroring #818's pair; the benefit and catalogue `SELECT`s gained the price/tax columns so no per-plan round trip was added. No migration, nothing persisted, nothing charged, and the Example Timeline is untouched. Admin: `section_billing_event_simulation` is declared last in `PLAN_SECTION_ORDER` and rendered by `plans/PlanBillingEventSimulation.tsx`, which formats and never recomputes. Tests: `api/src/test/plan-billing-event-simulation.test.ts` (11 unit), the engine's own `minimumCycles` block in `billing-simulation.test.ts` (4), `apps/admin/src/test/plan-billing-event-simulation.test.ts` (7) and integration coverage of the route in `membership-plans.test.ts` (6).)

- **Done (continued)**: #916 (**One column grid for a Membership Plan's Product sections, with the original and final price.** The three Benefit sections (One-off / Session / Period) were three independent tables sized by their own content, so `QUANTITY`, `FREQUENCY` and `BENEFIT` landed at a different horizontal position in each one and the Products could not be read as one data set — and none of them said what an item costs. The columns are now declared **once**, as `SELLABLE_ITEM_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` in `apps/admin/src/components/SellableItemBenefits.tsx`, in the order the ticket fixes: Product, Quantity, Frequency, **Benefit**, Original Price, Final Price — Benefit between Frequency and the prices, never after them. `sellableItemBenefitColumns()` takes the flags from the *page*, not from the section, so every section of one card gets the same grid; the read-only view renders a `table-layout: fixed` table from that declaration with one `<colgroup>`, so a long item name can no longer widen its column, and the table scrolls (`overflow-x: auto`, #637's answer) instead of squashing. `showFrequency` is true for all three Plan sections now: a One-off item's Frequency cell holds a `—` rather than disappearing and shifting everything after it. The two prices are **the server's**: a new pure module `api/src/domain/planBenefitPrices.ts` reports the item's gross unit price, the same price after the row's own `(action, value)` treatment, and the two line totals beside them — every figure from `applyLineBenefit()` (#896), the very function the Billing Simulation's charge builders call, so the Benefit sections and #915's Billing Event Simulation on the same card cannot quote one line two ways (an integration test asserts they agree to the cent). The gross-up is `computePriceFields()`'s, extracted as `grossBenefitUnitPrice()` and shared with #915's own loader, so no page does tax arithmetic (#817) and an item with no price reads `—` rather than €0.00. `enrichPlan` and the three section `GET`/`PUT`s all report the pair, and a Mandatory item the Plan has no stored row for yet (#893's `implicit: true`) is priced exactly like a configured one — `loadMandatorySellableItems()` carries the price columns for it. Nothing is stored and nothing is charged: like `example_timeline` and `billing_event_simulation`, the amounts are computed on every read. No migration. The Promotions card is deliberately untouched — a Promotion's grants are priced against the assignment they are applied to, which is #919/#920's question. Tests: `api/src/test/plan-benefit-prices.unit.test.ts` (9), a `#916` block in `api/src/test/membership-plan-benefits.test.ts` (10 integration) and `apps/admin/src/test/plan-benefit-columns.test.ts` (27).)

- **Done (continued)**: #919/#920 (**One column grid for a Promotion's Product sections, with the regular and final price, and a Membership Fee Promotion duration that stops claiming to be months.** #920 is #919 plus the two price columns, so one PR answers both. The three Promotion sections (Session / One-off / Periodical) were the mirror image of the defect #916 fixed on the Membership Plan card: `showFrequency` was true for the Periodical section alone, so `QUANTITY` and `PROMOTION` sat at a different horizontal position in each section and the three could not be read as one table. They now render from the very same `SELLABLE_ITEM_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` declaration with the same flags for every section — Product, Quantity, Frequency, **Promotion**, Regular Price, Final Price, Promotion between Frequency and the prices — so a Session or One-off item whose Product carries no frequency keeps its cell with a `—` instead of shifting the columns after it. No second declaration and no second grid: the shared component already took the flags from the page. The two prices are **the server's** and are not a second pricing implementation either — #916's wiring moved out of `membership-plans.ts` into `api/src/api/sellable-item-benefit-pricing.ts` (`grossBenefitUnitPrice()` over `computePriceFields()`, `withSellableItemBenefitPrices()` over `domain/planBenefitPrices.ts` and `applyLineBenefit()`), and `promotion-details.ts` is now its second caller with `context: 'promotion'`, which is what lets a Promotion quote the two amount-taking actions (`fixed_discount`, `fixed_price`) a Plan may not configure (#896 section 16). A Promotion section therefore cannot price an item differently from the Plan section beside it: same gross-up, same treatment arithmetic, VAT-inclusive, `—` rather than EUR 0.00 for an item with no price, and the line total shown beneath the unit price whenever the quantity makes the two differ. The price columns come from the benefit row's own join, so a Promotion still prices an item that has since been deactivated. This narrows #916's "the Promotions card is deliberately untouched" note, which named these two tickets as the place to decide it; what is **not** narrowed is that an *applied* Promotion is still priced against the assignment it is applied to and from that application's own snapshot (#635 section 16) — these two columns are a catalogue-side preview of what the Promotion grants, nothing an assignment reads. Nothing is stored and nothing is charged; no migration. The Membership Fee Promotion keeps its own five-column grid and its own data model, with `DURATION (MONTHS)` renamed to `DURATION` in both halves of the section (the already-present `promotions.col_duration` key; the two `(months)` keys are gone). Terminology only — the stored `duration_months` is still a count of calendar months bounded by the Promotion's own timeline (#625, #892, #899), and no value was converted. Tests: `api/src/test/promotion-benefit-prices.test.ts` (10 integration) and `apps/admin/src/test/promotion-benefit-columns.test.ts` (11), plus the updated Promotions block in `apps/admin/src/test/plan-benefit-columns.test.ts`.)

- **Done (continued)**: #922 (**Billing Event Simulation on Promotions.** The Promotion card gains the third projection #915 gave the Membership Plan card, over the items a Promotion affects: one group per **billing date**, each line showing the Regular Price beside the Final Price the Promotion's own `(action, value)` pair gives it (#896), with a per-date total and VAT-inclusive amounts. It is the *same* projection rather than a second one — the ticket's load-bearing clause is "do not create a second pricing engine [...] the only difference should be the input/context" — so #915's shared parts (the result shape, the `SIMULATED_CYCLES` horizon floor, the group-by-date re-grouping) moved into the new pure `api/src/domain/billingEventSimulation.ts` and each entity keeps only its adapter: `promotionBillingEventSimulation.ts` expresses the Promotion as the hypothetical assignment of a member given it today and hands it to `computeBillingSimulation()`, so every date, covered unit/period and amount is the engine's — the code the nightly run prices an applied Promotion with. A `Once`/`Per Session` grant appears once for its whole quantity; a periodical grant recurs at its own frequency, discounted for the periods its quantity covers and at the regular price afterwards; frequencies stay independent and only coincide when their dates do. **No Membership Fee line**: a Promotion has no fee of its own (it belongs to the Membership Plan the Promotion is applied alongside), so inventing one would advertise a price no member is promised — what a Promotion does to a fee is the Example Timeline beside it, and the disclaimer says so. Served by `GET /promotions/:id/billing-event-simulation`, loaded with the card's other sub-resources and refreshed whenever a Benefit section is saved; the grant prices come from #919/#920's own `grossBenefitUnitPrice()`, so the two halves of one card cannot quote an item two ways. Read-only, persisted nowhere, charges nothing, no migration. Admin: #915's component moved to the shared `apps/admin/src/components/BillingEventSimulation.tsx` and both cards render it, with the wire shape and price-label mapping in the JSX-free `lib/billingEventSimulation.ts` and every label resolved in the calling page's namespace.)

- **Done (continued)**: #926 (**`Applies To` on a Promotion — Membership Plan or Product.** A Promotion was implicitly always about a Membership Plan: it carried a Membership Fee Benefit and a set of Suitable Membership Plans, and both sections were shown whatever the gym was actually configuring. `promotions.applies_to` (migration 204, `VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'membership_plan'` + `chk_promotions_applies_to`) makes the target explicit, and the editor's new `APPLIES TO` radio group hides the two Membership-Plan-specific sections — **Membership Fee Promotion** and **Suitable Membership Plans** — while the target is `Sellable Item`. The accepted set is declared once in `api/src/domain/promotionTarget.ts` and mirrored for the radio group in `apps/admin/src/lib/promotionTargets.ts` (a unit test asserts the two lists, the default and the locale keys agree), so a new target goes in those two places plus the CHECK. **Configuration only**: nothing reads the column — no apply, pricing, snapshot or eligibility path — so every existing Promotion keeps behaving exactly as it did and the default backfills every row to `membership_plan` in one statement. Switching the target **migrates and reinterprets nothing** (the ticket's §4): the `promotion_membership_fee_benefits` row and the `promotion_membership_plans` rows are left exactly as stored, so switching back shows what was there before — which is why `PUT /promotions/:id` writes this column alone and why the admin's main save *skips* the replace-all `PUT /:id/plans` while the target is `sellable_item` instead of sending the hidden draft (a `[]` there would silently discard the gym's selection). Both halves of the card ask one predicate (`targetsMembershipPlan()`), so a hidden section cannot be visible in the read-only view and missing from the form; the radio updates the card at once and closes the Membership Fee editor it hides; `⋮ → Details` reports the target too; `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it. The Example Timeline, Billing & Duration, the three Product Benefit sections and the Billing Event Simulation are **not** hidden: the first two are the Promotion's own Free/Paid/Bonus timeline (which also bounds a grant's coverage) and the last carries no Membership Fee line by design (#922). Out of scope per the ticket: multiple Products, bundles, a per-item benefit for the target item, a Billing Event Simulation for a Product Promotion and any pricing change. Tests: `api/src/test/promotion-target.unit.test.ts` (9) and an `applies_to` block in `api/src/test/promotions.test.ts` (9 integration).)

- **Done (continued)**: #928 (**The Members list is the same list as Products.** Members was the last staff list still rendered by `DataTable`: its column widths were per-cell hints with nothing underneath them, and its five filters sat unlabelled in a page-level toolbar above the table, so the screen read as a different design system from Products and Training Plans beside it. It now wears the shared shape and nothing else changed: the column titles and every collapsed row are laid out from one `LIST_COLUMNS` declaration (Name, Email, NIF/NIE/Passport, Payment, Enrollment, Actions — the same columns, in the same order, showing the same values), header band and rows share that grid inside one `overflow-x: auto` wrapper so a long name or email can no longer push a row's values out of line with its header (#637), and the surface, band, cell insets and dividers come from `listChrome.ts` — the ones `DataTable` itself is built from (#724) — rather than a copy, so the list cannot drift from the tables that still use it. The five filters move into `FilterBar`/`FilterField` with `filterControlStyle`, each one carrying its label instead of relying on a placeholder (two new keys, `members.filter_search` and `members.filter_document`, in en/es/ca; the other three labels already existed); what each filter *does* — the query params, the URL sync, the Center filter appearing only for a gym with more than one center — is untouched. The whole collapsed row is the expand control, as on Products, and stays keyboard-operable the way the chevron button it replaces was (`role="button"`, `tabIndex`, `aria-expanded`, Enter/Space); the chevron is decorative beside the ⋮ menu in the Actions cell, since a second control nested inside a `role="button"` row is not one. `+ Add Member` drops the page's own copy of `btnStyle` for the shared helper at the same colour the `+ Add Sellable Item` button uses. Everything else is deliberately as it was: the expanded body is still the recessed `listExpandedStyle` surface holding the read-only Profile and, behind `⋮ → Edit`, the inline form (#797/#882); creation is still the modal it was (inline creation is #805's shape for other pages, not this ticket's); the ⋮ menu, its order and every action are unchanged. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no new component. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/members-list-ui.test.ts` (19), which pins both halves — the new structure, and the columns, values, filters, actions and expand/collapse that must not have moved with it.)

- **Done (continued)**: #929 (**The Member card wears the application's own chrome, in both modes.** #928 brought the Members *list* onto the shared card list; inside an expanded Member the card was still its own design system. Nine files — the read-only body, the inline Edit form, the Profile layout and the six sections — had each grown a private copy of the same objects, with the numbers drifting between them: two spellings of the uppercase section header, inputs at 13px on a 6px radius in one section and 14px on a 4px radius in the next, three different hairline greys, and Save buttons in a hardcoded lilac, a black box and a green no Theme could reach. `apps/admin/src/components/formChrome.ts` is now the one declaration of all of it — the two heading levels, a section's spacing and the rule that separates it from the section above, the card a section's contents sit in, the inline editor box, the muted/hint text, the field label, the single control box an `<input>`/`<select>`/`<textarea>` all wear, the read-only value box, the checkbox row, the help/field-error/form-error lines, the two action rows, the neutral half of a button pair, the dashed `+ Add` and the in-card text link — and every Member card file spreads it, so the card cannot drift from itself or from Products, whose inline form's numbers it takes. Three visible consequences: a read-only value now sits exactly where its input sits (`formValueStyle` carries `formControlStyle`'s padding, type size and transparent border of the same width — every value used to jump 10px sideways as `⋮ → Edit` opened); the sections are separated by the card's own themed hairline, with the first carrying none, so the card reads as one structure rather than a stack of independent fields (§4); and the Profile's two modes render the same section header and the same card around the same layout (`MemberProfileLayout`, #882), with the Edit form's Save moved onto the Theme's primary-button colours (#912) and its Cancel onto the matching secondary — as were the Extend expiration and Book pairs. Every border, background and link colour that had a Theme variable now reads it, with the old literal as the `var()` fallback, so nothing looks different until a gym themes it. Frontend only, and deliberately nothing else: no data, no API, no validation rule, no permission, no new field and no change to the Members list or its filters — the ticket's `New Member` badge is #927's field and is not invented here. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/member-card-chrome.test.ts` (8), which lists every file the card is built from and fails if the directory grows one that is not listed, asserts none of them restates a section header, an input border, a card border or a primary colour, and pins the shared objects' own relationships; #882's layout test keeps passing with its PROFILE-section locator relaxed to match the opening tag now that the first section carries `divider={false}`.)

- **Done (continued)**: #930 (**Center selection is the Calendar's, not the application header's.** `CenterSelector` sat in `TopHeader`, so every section of the admin app showed a gym-wide `All centers` dropdown — which read as a selection they all obeyed, and the Members list never did: it has had its own Center filter in its own filter bar all along, and the header control changed nothing on that page. The dropdown is deleted from the header (the component with it) and its one control is now `[locale]/calendar/CalendarCenterFilter.tsx`, in the Calendar's filter bar, wearing `FilterField` + `filterControlStyle` like any other page filter rather than the header band's translucent box. The *state* is deliberately unchanged: it is still the app's one `CenterContext`, because that is what `apiClient` sends as `x-center-id` and what `resolveCenterId()` defaults a write's center from (class sessions, spaces, rooms, trainer availability) and what `ThemeProvider` paints a Center's own theme from — forking it into page-local state would have cost a multi-center gym both. What is new is that the selection now narrows the calendar itself, which the header dropdown never did: the page passes `center_id` to `GET /class-sessions` (which already accepted it) and to `GET /calendar-events`, which gains the filter — a manual event whose `center_id IS NULL` is gym-wide and stays visible under every center, mirroring #478's rule on the member side, while a session's center is mandatory so that filter is strict. Nothing renders for a single-center gym, as before. Out of scope and untouched: the Members list and all five of its filters, member↔center assignment, every permission and `centerContext`'s own `allowedCenterIds` rules. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/calendar-center-filter.test.ts` (19) pins both halves — no header selector and no second copy of the control, the Calendar's filter and its query param, and the Members-side filter that must not have moved with it — plus a `GET /calendar-events?center_id` block in `api/src/test/calendar-events.test.ts` (5) covering the narrowing, the gym-wide row, combination with the other filters and tenant isolation.)

- **Done (continued)**: #931 (**Promotions removed from Members.** A Promotion applies to a target entity — a **Membership Plan** or a **Product** — so #634 §13's Member-level `PROMOTIONS` section was the wrong place for it: a Member benefits from a Promotion because of what they are assigned or buy, never because one was attached to them directly. The section is gone from the Member card (`MemberPromotions.tsx` deleted, with its `+ Add Promotion` button, its `No promotions applied` message, its Plan→Promotion picker and the seventeen `members.promotions_*`/`section_promotions` keys in en/es/ca), and `GET /user-memberships/member/:memberId/configuration` no longer reports a `promotions` array — which also drops one `fetchAppliedPromotions()` round trip per live assignment from every Member card expansion. The Member-level read is now `{plans, services}`; `new_member_eligible` stays on each plan, since it is a property of the Member and the apply paths are its enforcement point either way. Deliberately unchanged, because this is where the Promotion still lives: `promotions` and every `promotion_*` table, `POST`/`DELETE /user-memberships/:id/promotions`, `applyPromotionToMembership()`, `validatePromotionSelection()`, the #628 Promotions section of the **Assign New Plan** editor (the Promotions a plan is assigned *with* — the indirect path the ticket describes), `POST /membership-plans/:id/assign`'s auto-apply, the Assigned Plans card's own applied-Promotion cards (`AssignedPlanPromotions.tsx`, read from each application's own snapshot, #635 §16) and the Member's Billing Simulation, which still shows the discounts an applied Promotion produces. No migration and no data touched: every `user_membership_promotions` row and snapshot stays exactly as it was. Tests: new `apps/admin/src/test/member-promotions-removed.test.ts` (9) pins the absence — the deleted component, the dropped keys in all three locales, no apply call from any Member surface — alongside what must survive (the Assign New Plan selection, the Assigned Plans card, the Promotions page); `api/src/test/member-membership-configuration.test.ts` keeps applying promotions in its fixtures and asserts the payload has no `promotions` key while the rows are still in the database; `member-membership-sections.test.ts` and `member-card-chrome.test.ts` drop to the three remaining sections.)

- **Done (continued)**: #942 (**A session package's Price reads as the package total, not a per-session price.** A Product of Type `Sessions` stores the total price of the whole package, but the list put `Units: 5` and `Price: €50.00` in adjacent columns with nothing saying which of the two readings was right — €50.00 for the five, or €50.00 each and €250.00 the lot. Every surface that shows such a price now carries the sentence under it: *Total price for 5 sessions*, pluralised in ICU off the item's own `units` (`1 session` / `5 sessions`, AC 5), with *Total price for the complete package* as the wording for a session item whose nullable `units` is unset. Five call sites — the collapsed row's Price cell, the read-only expanded card, the Details modal and the Price input of **both** the inline create card and the inline editor (#805) — and one rule behind them: the pure `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/financials/sellable-items/sellableItemPriceNotes.ts`, so no page spells out `type === 'sessions'` beside a price for itself. The two form hints read the *draft's* live Type and Units rather than the saved row, so the sentence appears the moment `Sessions` is chosen and follows the count as it is typed — the person entering the figure is the one who most needs to know what it buys. The read-only card and the Details modal also gained the `(tax included)` / `(tax excluded)` suffix the list has always had (`taxNoteKey()`, which answers `null` for an item with no tax rate rather than claiming a tax that is not configured — the same condition the list already uses to choose between `amount_incl_tax` and the raw amount). **Labels only**: not one displayed figure moves, nothing multiplies a price by a unit count, both forms still submit `amount` unscaled, and there is no migration and no API change (AC 1, AC 8). Fee-based items are untouched — `sessionPackageNote()` answers `null` for every other type (AC 7). The Price column widens from 150px to 200px, since every cell on the #637 grid is nowrap-and-ellipsis and the sentence has to fit in en/es/ca. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/sellable-items-session-package-price.test.ts` (30) covers the pure rule's boundaries (1 / 5 / 10 units, null / 0 / negative / NaN / fractional, every other type), the tax suffix including the zero-rate and no-rate cases, the ICU plural in all three locales, a guard that no wording in any locale reads as a per-session price, and the five render sites.)

- **Done (continued)**: #927 (**The Member's automatically calculated `New Member` status.** A Member is a New Member when they have not had an active Membership Plan during the previous **six** months. The calculation already existed — `domain/newMemberEligibility.ts`, #634 §3 — so the ticket added no second rule: it shortened that one window from twelve months to six (the answer on the thread was *"6 months here and in promotions"*, so the badge a gym reads and the window the four Promotion apply paths enforce are the same constant, which is §5's point in both directions) and exposed it per Member. `GET /members` now projects `is_new_member` for the whole page in **one** query (`newMemberStatusByMember()`, never one round trip per row and never a second SQL copy of the rule) and `GET /members/:id` reports the same field through `isNewMemberStatus()`; neither excludes anything, because a Member-level answer has no assignment being configured, so a Member holding a live plan reads `☐` and a Member with no assignment at all reads `☑`. Nothing is stored: no column, no migration, no writer — the status tracks the Membership history and the passing of the window on its own, and `PUT /members/:id`'s explicit field list cannot write it. On the admin side it is a **calculated field of the Profile**: `MEMBER_PROFILE_FIELDS` carries it with `calculated: true` and `MemberProfileLayout` renders such a field through its own `renderCalculated` callback in both modes, so the Edit form has no way to grow an input under that label, the key is absent from `MemberEditFormValues` and from the `PUT` payload, and reading and editing draw the same cell (`NewMemberValue`, a ticked or unticked box with an `aria-label` rather than a disabled checkbox). The member header carries the same value as an accent name-cell badge (#913's voice, `listNameBadgeAccentStyle`), so a new Member is identifiable without expanding the row. **This changes Promotion eligibility**: every Member who left between six and twelve months ago is now eligible for a new-members-only Promotion — the plain reading of the ticket, confirmed on the thread. Out of scope and untouched: Promotion configuration and its eligibility UI, Product and bundle Promotions, and any manual override. Tests: `api/src/test/new-member-eligibility.test.ts` grows the six-month window, the cutoff day itself and the Member-level "nothing excluded" case (22); an `is_new_member` block in `api/src/test/members.test.ts` (8) covers the ticket's four examples, both reads agreeing, per-Member answers in one page, that a `PUT` naming the field changes nothing, and that another gym's assignment never reaches it; and `apps/admin/src/test/member-new-member-status.test.ts` (12) pins the declaration, that there is nothing to type into or submit, the one value in both places and that the frontend derives no window of its own.)
- **Done (continued)**: #918 (**Frequency on Session Benefits.** A Membership Plan's Session Benefits could say *5 Personal Training Classes* but not *2 Personal Training Classes per week*, so a recurring session allowance was inexpressible. Each Session Benefit now carries a Frequency beside its quantity — `frequency` on `membership_plan_session` and on the assignment-side `user_membership_session` (migration 205), nullable with no default and no backfill, because `NULL` is the dropdown's `—` and is exactly what every benefit stored before the ticket means: a one-time allowance granted on the start date. It is **its own accepted set**, declared once in `api/src/domain/sessionBenefitFrequency.ts` (`once, week, four_weeks, month, year`), mirrored by `chk_<table>_frequency` and by `apps/admin/src/lib/sessionBenefitFrequency.ts` — three places, asserted by tests on both sides. `Weekly` is offered here although #821 retired `week` from `gym_charges.billing_frequency`: how often an allowance *renews* and how often an item is *priced* are different questions, which is the thread's Q2 answer ("It is the way to cover 1 session per week or 2 sessions per week. Products cannot be purchased per weeks"), and `per_session` is not a period so it is not offered. The replace-all `PUT` follows #896's three-way rule — a request that names no Frequency **keeps** the stored one, since a quantity-only save is what every client written before this ticket sends, and clearing it is the explicit `frequency: null` — and the value is **part of the agreement**: `snapshotAssignedPlan()` copies it onto the assignment and `writeAssignedPlanBenefitSection()` keeps it on a kept line, so a Plan switched from Weekly to Monthly afterwards cannot move what an existing member was agreed (#635 §13–§17). In the **simulation** the allowance is summarised onto the billing events that already exist rather than generating weekly ones (the thread's Q1 answer: "I don't expect having one per every week, instead I'd like to summarize it based on billing event frequency"): `buildSessionAllowanceStream()` runs the item at the *assignment's own* cadence and reports `quantity ×` the renewals falling in each cycle, so a 4-weekly Plan with `2 | Weekly | 50% discount` reads **8 sessions, €400 regular, €200 charged** on every billing date — the ticket's own worked example — while a monthly Plan reports the 5 renewals a 31-day cycle really contains and the 4 of the next, never a fractional 4.35. `once`, `—` and an assignment with no cadence keep the single start-date charge unchanged; a cycle with no renewal shows no line rather than "0 sessions"; and a Promotion's session grant still grants its units **in total** rather than per cycle (`allocateSessionUnits()` allocates the line's units as intervals with an offset for what earlier cycles consumed, which for a single charge is the pre-ticket arithmetic to the cent). `sessionFrequency` is a *required* field of `SimulationPlanBenefit` for the reason `benefit` and `personalFeeBenefit` are: a loader that forgot it would quietly show a member 2 sessions where their contract entitles them to 8. Admin: **one** column, not a second — the shared `SELLABLE_ITEM_BENEFIT_COLUMNS` Frequency cell takes a `frequencyColumn` prop (`'item'` by default, `'benefit'` only for the Plan's Session section), so the three sections stay the one aligned table #916 made them, and the Promotion sections are deliberately untouched. Still out of scope and still nobody's behaviour: a consumable allowance ledger — nothing tracks how many renewed sessions a member has used, and booking eligibility is unchanged. Tests: `api/src/test/session-benefit-frequency.unit.test.ts` (21, including the migration/domain list agreement and the renewal arithmetic), a `#918` block in `billing-simulation.test.ts` (6) and in `plan-billing-event-simulation.test.ts` (2), 10 integration cases in `membership-plan-benefits.test.ts`, 4 in `assigned-plan-snapshot.test.ts` (freeze, no drift, keep-on-edit, end-to-end simulation) and `apps/admin/src/test/session-benefit-frequency-ui.test.ts` (14).)
- **Done (continued)**: #945 (**Per Session removed from the Product Billing Frequency.** A session package's size is its **Units** and its Billing Frequency is when the whole package is billed — `Sessions / 10 units / €500 / Once` means €500 for the ten (#942) — so *Per Session* named a usage-based model the Product does not have. The selector now offers `—`, *Once*, *4 Weeks*, *Month*, *Year*. This is #821's retirement pattern applied a second time, which is the whole change: `per_session` moves from `OFFERED_SELLABLE_ITEM_FREQUENCIES` to `LEGACY_…` in `api/src/domain/sellableItemFrequency.ts` and in its form mirror, so `POST /sellable-items` refuses it, `PUT /sellable-items/:id` accepts it **only** on a row that already stores it (editing another field of a legacy item neither 400s nor rewrites its frequency), nothing may be moved onto it — `week` included — and `POST /:id/duplicate` copies it verbatim, because Duplicate is a copy and not the dropdown. **No migration and no backfill**: `gym_charges_billing_frequency_check` (migration 123) still permits all six values, so every existing row stays valid, reads back, classifies into the same benefit section (`per_session` is non-recurring, exactly as `once` is) and bills as before. The backfill §3 invites "where the intended behaviour is known" was declined although `cadenceForSellableItem()` gives `per_session` and `once` the same (absent) schedule: what a gym *meant* by the value is not knowable from the row, so the ticket's own fallback applies and the editor is where the correction happens — the retired value renders as a **disabled** option while the form holds it, with the notice beside it now **naming** that value (`frequency_legacy_notice` interpolates it in en/es/ca, since there are two retired values now; the key is resolved before `t()` is called, never through a `defaultValue` next-intl does not have). §2's "configuring a Product within other entities" needed nothing: the offered list has exactly one consumer and every other surface only *displays* a stored frequency, so a legacy row still reads *Per Session* where it is stored — pinned by a test. `week` stays **offered** by a Session Benefit's own Frequency (#918), which is a different question and a different set. §4: the Sessions model is untouched — no column, no API shape and no charge changes. Tests: `api/src/test/sellable-item-frequency.unit.test.ts` (13, both retired values in both directions), a `#945` block in `gym-charges.test.ts` (10 integration: the refused create, the ticket's own 10-session package on *Once*, a stored `per_session` item read, edited, duplicated and corrected, the one-way move, and the 400 naming the four offered values) and `apps/admin/src/test/sellable-item-frequency.test.ts` (15).)
- **Done (continued)**: #946 (**The Pre-paid Membership Fee is charged, not waived.** A Plan sold as *€70/month, Paid Duration 3, Pre-paid Duration 3* means the member pays €210 up front and is then charged nothing until the fourth period, but every prepaid period priced at 0 with a `waive` benefit: the Billing Event Simulation read `Membership Fee · Waived · €0.00` where the gym had sold €210, and — because `POST /payment-requests` refuses a cycle that owes nothing — a brand-new prepaid assignment could not raise its first payment at all, never stored a card and was skipped by the nightly run for ever. The whole Pre-paid Duration is now owed **once, on the first of its periods**, and the periods it pays for generate no Membership Fee event. One pure rule decides it, `prepaidPeriodsDueOn()` in `api/src/domain/planDuration.ts`, keyed on the *period* and never on the exact billing date (a first payment raised a day late still owes the same €210), clamped by the Paid Duration and counted in the assignment's own Billing Frequency periods (#892 — a 4-weekly Plan's two pre-paid periods are 56 days, not two months). It is read in exactly one place, `resolveMembershipFee()`, which is why no surface could be fixed on its own: the nightly run charges the lump on that cycle (`recurring_payment`, the amount in minor units at the provider boundary) and still records `waived_billing · prepaid_plan` for the cycles it covered, a staff- or member-raised payment request asks for it, and the Payments dashboard, My Membership, the Example Timeline and the Billing Event Simulation all quote it. Three properties keep the rest of the engine intact: the charge carries **no benefit line** (it is the regular price of the periods it pays for — it reports a `prepaid_periods` count instead, which is what the admin's *3 periods prepaid* note renders), a covered period answers `quantity: 0` and so reuses #918's existing "not a billing event" path rather than a new branch, and the charge stays `promotional` in the horizon's sense so a projection runs on to the first ordinary charge instead of stopping at the lump. A governing Promotion still decides the fee alone (#635 Q2) and the Personal Membership Fee Benefit still discounts every period the lump covers (#772). Admin: the Example Timeline's first Pre-paid row charges (`waived: false`, `€210.00 VAT included · 3 periods prepaid`, amber rather than the green "no charge" tone), the Billing Event Simulation's first group carries one fee line of `quantity: 3 / €210.00 / Regular price` and the Member card's own simulation notes the same count — every figure the server's, no arithmetic in any page (#817). No migration, no new endpoint, and nothing else about a billing event changed: Products, billing frequencies, benefits, waives, discounts, mandatory items, one-off and periodic items all behave exactly as before (§4). Tests: `api/src/test/plan-prepaid-first-charge.unit.test.ts` (19 — the rule's boundaries including the 4-weekly case and the Free Period offset, the fee resolver's lump / covered / resumed cycles, the Promotion precedence and the personal discount, and the ticket's own €70 × 3 example end to end through the Plan projection), updated prepaid expectations in `plan-prepaid-duration.test.ts`, `plan-example-timeline.test.ts`, `membership-plans.test.ts` and `billing-run-waived.test.ts` (where the prepaid cycle is now a 2 × 29.99 charge with the covered cycle waived beside it), and `apps/admin/src/test/prepaid-membership-fee-note.test.ts` (5).)
- **Done (continued)**: #947 (**The Nutrition Library is three catalogues: Foods, Personal Goals, Nutrition Goals.** Both libraries — a gym's `nutrition/nutrition-library` and Cordel's `cordel/nutrition-library` — are now three tabs over the same declaration, each tab an independent library with its own search, list, `+ Add` and row actions (§1, §2, §6, §7). Migration 206 adds `personal_goals` and `nutrition_goals`: **two tables, not one with a `kind` column**, because §8 keeps the concepts apart in the data model as well as the UI and §9 already has a Nutrition Goal eventually carrying a target value a Personal Goal never will. Ownership is the Foods library's, unchanged (§5): `gym_id IS NULL` is a platform-owned **System** row, read-only to every gym and administered from Cordel; a set `gym_id` is that gym's own, editable and soft-deletable (`status = 'deleted'`, with `deleted_at` and #799's three actor pairs). Because the two tables are identical in shape, there is **one router factory per side** — `goal-library.ts` mounted at `/personal-goals` and `/nutrition-goals`, `platform-goal-library.ts` at the `/platform` pair — over one declaration, `api/src/domain/goalLibrary.ts`, which owns the kinds, their tables, their audit entity types and the seeded rows; a third goal catalogue would be a line there plus a table, not a third router. Two details are load-bearing. Uniqueness is among **live** rows (a `live_name_key` VIRTUAL generated column non-NULL only while the row is not deleted, carrying the UNIQUE index — migration 183's device), so the index agrees with the routers' `status != 'deleted'` duplicate check and a name a gym deleted is re-usable rather than reserved for ever. And `slug` is a **System** row's label handle and only a System row's (`chk_<prefix>_slug_system_only`): the seven seeded rows of each kind are translated through `goal_library.<kind>_goal_<slug>` with the row's own `name` as the fallback — the `result_types` rule — which is why these catalogues need no per-locale junction table, and why neither router ever writes one. The slugs are the Nutrition Plan routers' own `NUTRITION_GOALS` vocabulary partitioned between the two concepts, plus **Fasting** (§4), so a later ticket can link a plan's goal to its catalogue row without a rename. **Nothing reads them yet**: assigning goals to plans or members and defining target values are explicitly out of scope, so `member_nutrition_plan_goals`, `nutrition_plan_template_goals` and the closed vocabulary they validate against are untouched, and Foods behaves exactly as before. Admin: `apps/admin/src/components/goalLibrary/` is the one declaration both pages import — the tab list, the four router roots, the row shape and payloads, the System-slug mirror, the tab strip, the Details modal and one `GoalLibrarySection` serving all four screens, which names no endpoint and decides no permission (#806) and keeps #797–#800's read-only-expanded / edit-behind-`⋮` model with inline create (#805), the shared filter bar, name badge and primary-button chrome (#724, #912, #929). New `goal_library` namespace in en/es/ca. Tests: `api/src/test/goal-library.test.ts` (72) and `platform-goal-library.test.ts` (54) — tenant isolation, auth and the role matrix, the System read-only 403s, the live-name uniqueness including re-creating a deleted name, the actor masking, validation and the `?status=deleted` listing; `goal-library-domain.unit.test.ts` (14) pins the declaration against the migration; `apps/admin/src/test/goal-library-ui.test.ts` (30) pins the tabs, the four roots against `app.ts`, the slug mirror, the label fallback, the read-only body and the locale keys.)
- **Done (continued)**: #954 (**Every primary action in the Admin app follows the Theme's Primary Button colour.** #901 made the subsection `Edit` button the first consumer of the Buttons group and #912 extended it to the two Theme screens — but the rest of the app still passed the legacy lilac literal to `btnStyle()`/`btnSmall()` at **56 call sites across 27 files**, so a gym that set *Primary Button = red* saw red on `Save changes` in the Theme editor and lilac on every other Save, Add, Create, New, Assign and Apply button in the product — including `CrudModal`'s shared Save, which every modal in the app renders. All of them now call `primaryBtnStyle()`/`primaryBtnSmall()`, and the two Calendar detail panels' full-width actions — which carry their own geometry rather than a helper's — spread `primaryActionColors` exactly where their `background: '#6c63ff', color: '#fff'` pair sat, so the trailing `opacity`/`cursor` overrides still win. **No new Theme token, no new helper, no geometry change** (§2, §4): the spread replaces two colour properties and nothing else, a `readOnlyStyle(primaryBtnStyle(), !canWrite)` dims the themed pair exactly as it dimmed the lilac, and no label, handler, `disabled` decision, permission gate, translation or locale key moved. Deliberately **not** converted (§5): the three file-picker controls — `ImageUploadField`, `ExerciseImageField`, `ExerciseVideoField` — because a picker is not its form's primary action, its Save is, and CLAUDE.md already rules that file-picker, secondary and destructive buttons keep their own colours; plus text links (whose token is the Links group's `--gd-link`, a separate decision), selected filter chips and selection/accent borders, which carry **state** rather than an action, and every `--brand` consumer, which is the sidebar's colour by design (§3). The enforcement is a scan rather than a list: `apps/admin/src/test/theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` walks every `.ts`/`.tsx` under `apps/admin/src` outside `src/test` and fails on a `btnStyle('#6c63ff')`, a `btnSmall('#6c63ff')` or a flat `background: '#6c63ff'` outside the three named pickers, and on any module but `ui.tsx` (and `themeTokens.ts`, which writes them) naming `--gd-primary-btn` for itself — so the next page with a Save button is told rather than trusted. It also pins the live-preview link §6 asks for: `applyTokens()` writes the edited Buttons group into exactly the two variables the helpers read, which is why the Theme editor needs no reload. Frontend only: no migration, no API change, no member app. Tests: 9 added to `theme-primary-buttons.test.ts` (19 total); `members-list-ui.test.ts`'s `+ Add` assertion moved with the helper it pins. Admin suite green at 1,571.)
- **Done (continued)**: #955 (**The Billing Event Simulation is collapsible billing-period cards.** The section was a vertically stacked list of every billing event with a `Total` row under each date's lines, which on a Plan carrying four Products filled a screen before the second billing date appeared. It is now one **collapsible card per billing date**: the header carries the date, how many lines fall on it and that date's total — so the per-date `Total` row is gone, the total moved into the header — the lines appear underneath when the card is open, and a single *Expand all* / *Collapse all* control sits above them, rendered only when there is more than one period and always reflecting whether every card is currently open. The first period is expanded and the rest collapsed, each card still toggles on its own, and the whole header is the control (a `<button>` carrying `aria-expanded`, the shape the list rows use, with the chevron decorative beside it). The detail table is deliberately **not** a new design: `ExampleTimeline.tsx` now exports its header and body cells (`timelineThStyle`/`timelineTdStyle`) and its three tones (`TIMELINE_TONE_BACKGROUND`/`TIMELINE_TONE_TEXT`) and the simulation imports them, so Date / Billing Event / Status / Amount reads in the same row density, borders, typography and colour semantics as the Example Timeline above it on the same card, and no hex carrying a billing meaning is declared twice (a test asserts the component contains none of the five). Which tone a line reads in is the new pure `simulationLineTone()` beside the wire shape — green for a `waive`d or `included` line, amber for any other action and for #946's `prepaid_periods` lump, grey for an ordinary charge — taken from the treatment the server reported and never from the amount, since €0.00 is also what an item with no price costs. The open/closed state is pure too (`initialExpandedPeriods()`, `allExpandedPeriods()`, `everyPeriodExpanded()`), which is what makes the ticket's interaction rules testable in an app with no component-test infra, and it is re-seeded only when the set of billing dates itself changes, so the Promotion card's late-arriving projection cannot close a card the viewer has just opened. **One presentation, every consumer**: both cards already rendered the one shared `apps/admin/src/components/BillingEventSimulation.tsx` (#922), so the Membership Plan and Promotion sections are converted by converting it and neither page gained a card, a toggle or a table of its own. The ticket's third context, an **Assigned Membership Plan**, has no Billing Event Simulation on `main` to convert — #924 stage 3 is adding a *Membership Fee* Simulation (the Example Timeline) to that card, and the Member's own consolidated Billing Simulation (#629) is a different section with its own grouping, per-section totals and grand total — so it inherits these cards the moment it renders this component, which is what the shared component is for. Presentation only, as §"Preserve existing business logic" requires: no API, no migration, no new endpoint, nothing persisted, and every date, amount, treatment and total still the server's — expanding a card fetches nothing and recomputes nothing. Seven new keys per namespace in en/es/ca (`simulation_items_count` ICU-pluralised, the two control labels and the four column headers), none of them saying "Benefit" in the Promotions namespace (#896 §3). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/billing-event-simulation-cards.test.ts` (20) — the tone rule including the waive-wins and €0-is-not-free cases, the three state helpers, one component for both consumers, the borrowed palette, the total in the header, the `aria-expanded` control, the single-period case and the labels in both namespaces — with the two existing simulation files' "no arithmetic in the page" assertions still passing unchanged.)
- **Done (continued)**: #957 (**“Add Product”: edit-only, themed, and named Product.** The Member card's ADDITIONAL SERVICES section offered its add action as a lilac text link that was present whether the card was being read or edited — so an expanded Member row carried a write affordance (#797 says expanding reads and `⋮ → Edit` writes), and a gym that set *Buttons → Primary Button* saw the colour everywhere except here. The action is now a real button wearing `primaryBtnSmall()` — the same Theme pair #912/#954 gave every other primary action, no new style, no new token and no hardcoded lilac — and the shared editor (`financials/assigned-plans/AdditionalPeriodicServices.tsx`, rendered by both the Assigned Plan card and the Member card) takes a `canAdd` prop, default `true`: the Member card passes its existing Edit-mode flag, so the button is **absent** in the read-only view rather than disabled, while the Assigned Plans card — which has no edit mode — passes nothing and keeps the action it always had. The copy is the **final Product terminology** the ticket's implementation note asks for (*Additional Products*, *Additional Periodic Products*, *+ Add Product*, *Select a product…*, and the needs-a-plan line) in en/es/ca, so no screen shows an intermediate "Add Service" state while #949 is still unanswered. Only the locale **values** moved — the `services_*` keys are identifiers and renaming those is #949 stage 2's, which is also why this does not pre-empt #924 stage 5's §11 rename of the same section. *Professional Services* (`pt_slots_no_services`) and the persisted audit action `add_service` are deliberately untouched, as is the `Sellable Item` column header, which names the entity #949 owns. Frontend only: no API, no migration, no payload, no billing rule, and the table, the removal action and the needs-a-plan line all still read in both modes (§"Preserve"). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/add-product-edit-mode.test.ts` (10 — the render gate and its `true` default, absent-not-disabled, the themed helper and the three lilac forms it must not carry, the preserved read-only affordance, and every section string in all three locales saying "product" and not "servic"/"servei").
- **Done (continued)**: #960 (**`New Member` reads as a Yes/No chip on the label's own row.** #927 drew the Member Profile's calculated status as a ticked or unticked box (`☑` / `☐`) in the box an `<input>` occupies, so one boolean cost the card a label, a 14px gap and a full value box of height — and a glyph is a worse answer than a word for a field nobody can change. The value is now a compact chip beside its label: *New Member  Yes*. **No colour and no shape are declared for it**: `newMemberChipStyle()` spreads the list's own metadata pill (#724) — the accent voice (#913) for `Yes`, which is literally the pill the Members list already puts beside a new member's name, and the neutral one for `No` — overriding nothing but the left margin the name cell wanted, so the badge in the row and the value in the card below it read as one statement and a test pins both halves to `listChrome.ts`. The layout change is the shared `MemberProfileLayout`'s, not either half's: a `calculated` field renders label-and-value inline while an editable one keeps the stacked shape, because that stacked box is where its `<input>` sits in the other mode (#929) — so the read-only Profile and the Edit form still place the field identically, still walk `MEMBER_PROFILE_FIELDS` once, and the Edit form still has no way to grow a control under it (#927 §4). Accessibility is the chip's `aria-label`, which keeps #927's full sentence (*New Member: yes*) rather than leaving a screen reader with a bare "Yes" next to a label that is not programmatically tied to it; the two keys are resolved by `newMemberValueKey()` / `newMemberAnnounceKey()` in `memberProfile.ts` so neither half picks its own wording. Presentation only, as the ticket requires: `is_new_member` is still the server's one calculation (`NEW_MEMBER_WINDOW_MONTHS`, #927), nothing is submitted, no API, no migration, no member app, and `members.yes` / `members.no` are the only new keys (en/es/ca, the per-namespace convention `plans`, `promotions` and `sellable_items` already follow). Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/member-new-member-chip.test.ts` (10 — no glyph left anywhere, the chip is read-only and declares no hex, both pills come from `listChrome`, the inline row vs the stacked editable field, one field set in both modes, and the labels in all three locales), with #927's own file updated to the chip and its label check extended to the two new keys. Admin suite green at 1,616.)
- **Done (continued)**: #962 (**“Example Timeline” reads “Membership Fee Simulation”.** The section on the Membership Plan card and the Promotion card projects what the *Membership Fee* does over the periods of a contract, which is not what "Example Timeline" says — and the Assigned Plan card has called the very same projection **Membership Fee Simulation** since #924 stage 3, so one screen's name for it disagreed with another's. Both headings now read *Membership Fee Simulation* / *Simulación de la Cuota de Membresía* / *Simulació de la Quota de Membresia*, word for word the strings `assigned_plans_page.section_fee_simulation` already held, and the key is `section_fee_simulation` in all three namespaces: `plans.section_example_timeline` and `promotions.section_timeline` are **retired** rather than left holding the new label, so a grep for the old wording finds nothing. The two empty states that told a gym to configure something "to preview an example timeline" name the simulation instead (`plans.timeline_unavailable`, `promotions.timeline_empty`); every other string of the section — the row statuses, the `Example: a member starting on {date}` note, the disclaimers — is untouched, because "Example" there qualifies the *dates*, which really are illustrative. A label rename and nothing else, as the ticket requires: no API change, no migration, no new endpoint, no locale key added, and not one date, amount, period status or billing calculation moved. The projection keeps its own names on purpose — `api/src/domain/planExampleTimeline.ts`, the `example_timeline` wire field, `GET /membership-plans/:id/example-timeline` and the shared `components/ExampleTimeline.tsx` are what `CLAUDE.md` pins by name, and renaming a module to rename a heading is how a rename becomes a refactor. Tests: `apps/admin/src/test/membership-fee-simulation-label.test.ts` (7 — one key and one wording per locale across the three namespaces, both old keys gone, no "example timeline" wording left in *any* admin locale string in any of the three languages, the three pages rendering that key, the declared section slot unchanged, and the domain names still in place), with `plan-example-timeline`, `plans-expanded-read-only` and `plan-billing-event-simulation` updated to the new key.)
- **Done (continued)**: #1009 (**Marking a workout block complete works again, and the block-level result snapshot is gone.** `POST /me/workout-block-logs` read `wb.result_type` — a column migration 074 (#154) dropped when it moved the result type from the block down to the exercise instance — so **every** member who marked a block complete got a `500`, and the endpoint had no test, which is why it went unnoticed.
